y=\sin(\mathbf{x}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*})+\varepsilon
where
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,0.1)
.

We highlight that the mechanism driving this difference may be different in
these two examples: Both examples train SGD/SME for
T=4
epochs, and
then continue to apply gradient flow from the last SGD/SME iterate until
convergence. The dynamics over the first
T=4
epochs in Figure
2

diverge between SGD and SME, but converge again upon
the subsequent application of gradient flow. This suggests that SGD and SME
reach basins of attraction of (local) minimizers of the empirical risk with
similar statistical properties, and that their differences may be driven by
different oscillatory behaviors around these minimizers.
In contrast, the difference between SGD and SME in
Figure
3
persists even through the later gradient flow training,
with SGD achieving a higher overlap
d^{-1}\bm{\theta}^{t\top}\bm{\theta}^{*}

and smaller squared norm
d^{-1}\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|^{2}
. This suggests that SGD and
SME reach local minimizers of the empirical risk that have different
generalization properties. This difference is reflected (over
the first 4 training epochs) by the DMFT theory.

The small learning rate limit.

Figure
4
compares the overlap dynamics of SGD and SME in units
of rescaled time
\tau=\eta t
across various learning rates
\eta
for the Tanh
activation setting of Figure
2
, verifying the claim in Section

2.5.1
that these dynamics
converge to a common (gradient flow) limit as
\eta\to 0
.

Figure 4.
Effect of learning rate on SGD vs. SME divergence.
Overlap dynamics are plotted in units of rescaled time
\tau=\eta t
, for
various learning rates
\eta\in\{0.5,1.25,2.5\}
. At small learning rates
(
\eta=0.5
), the SME diffusion provides an accurate approximation of SGD, and
their gap widens as
\eta
increases.

Figure 5.
Convergence to one-pass SGD in the large sample limit.
Overlap dynamics are plotted in units of rescaled time
\tau=\gamma t
, fixing

d=4000
and varying
\gamma=n/d\in\{0.5,1.0,5.0,20.0\}
.
As the training sample size
\gamma
increases, multi-pass SGD dynamics (solid
lines) and their DMFT predictions (dotted lines) both converge to the simulated
overlap statistics of one-pass SGD (black dashed line).

Transition to one-pass SGD dynamics.

Figure
5
compares the overlap dynamics of SGD in units of
rescaled time
\tau=\gamma t
for various dimension ratios
\gamma=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}n/d
, verifying the claim in Section
2.5.2

that as
\gamma\to\infty
, these overlap curves converge to a limit that
represents one-pass SGD, and the training time at which these curves plateau
is delayed.

3.
Existence and uniqueness of the DMFT fixed
point

In the remainder of the paper, we prove Theorems
2.5

and
2.6
.

This section defines the spaces
\mathcal{S}\equiv\mathcal{S}(T,C_{0})
and
\mathcal{S}^{\text{cont}}\equiv\mathcal{S}^{\text{cont}}(T,C_{0})
, and proves
2.5
. The
proof is an adaptation of
[
18
]
for gradient flow (see also
[
30
]
in a
setting of Langevin dynamics), and will consist of:

(1)

Defining spaces
\mathcal{S}_{\xi}

for
(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}
for
(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})
,
and showing that on these spaces, the primary processes

\theta^{t},\xi^{t}
and auxiliary response processes
r_{\theta}^{t,s},r_{f}^{t,*},r_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})
are well-defined.

(2)

Showing that the transformations which represent the DMFT fixed point
equations for
(C_{\theta},R_{\theta},C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)
are contractive in a suitable
metric.

The main differences between our argument and those of
[
18
]
involve the
treatment of the stochastic Volterra-type integrals in (
14
),
(
16
), and (
17
) that arise
due to the stochastic gradient noise in both SGD and SME; this necessitates
modified definitions and treatments of the response processes, as discussed in
Remark
2.4
.

3.1.
Definition of the space
\mathcal{S}

We remind the reader that we abbreviate
\|\cdot\|
for the
\ell_{2}
-norm of
a vector and Frobenius norm of a matrix, and write
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{op}}
for the

\ell_{2}
-to-
\ell_{2}
matrix operator norm.

For any Euclidean space
E
, we denote by

L^{4}([0,t],E)
the space of (Borel-measurable) functions

s\in[0,t]\mapsto x^{s}\in E
for which

\|x\|_{L^{4}}:=\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|x^{s}\|_{2}^{4}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/4}<\infty.

For a linear operator
R:L^{4}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{k})\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
, we define also
its operator norm

\|R\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\sup_{x\in L^{4}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{k}):\|x\|_{L^{4}}\leq 1}\|R(x)\|_{2}.

We say that
R
is a bounded linear operator if
\|R\|_{\mathrm{op}}<\infty
.

Fix any
T>0
. Under Assumptions
2.1
and
2.3
, there
exist constants
M_{f},M_{g},M_{\eta}>0
such that

(55)

\begin{gathered}\sup_{(\xi,w^{*},\varepsilon)\in\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{k^{*}}\times\mathbb{R}}\{\|f(\xi,w^{*},\varepsilon)\|,\|\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi,w^{*},\varepsilon)\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\mathrm{D}_{w^{*}}f(\xi,w^{*},\varepsilon)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\}\leq M_{f},\\
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\|\mathrm{D}g(\theta)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq M_{g},\qquad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bar{\eta}^{t}\cdot\max\{1,1/\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}}\}\leq M_{\eta}.\end{gathered}

For a constant
C_{0}>0
(which we will take large enough, depending on
T
and
the above constants
M_{f},M_{g},M_{\eta}
),
we define envelope functions
\Phi_{R_{\theta}},\Phi_{R_{f}},\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}}:[0,T]\to[0,\infty)
by

(56)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t)

\displaystyle=C_{0}\left({\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{R_{f}}(t-r)\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(r)\mathrm{d}r}\right),\quad\text{ with }\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(0)=C_{0},

(57)

\displaystyle\Phi_{R_{f}}(t)

\displaystyle=C_{0}\left({\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-r)\Phi_{R_{f}}(r)\mathrm{d}r+\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t)}\right),

(58)

\displaystyle\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}}(t)

\displaystyle=C_{0}\left(1+\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-r)\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}}(r)\mathrm{d}r\right).

We then define an envelope function
\Phi_{C_{\theta}}:[0,T]\to[0,\infty)
by

(59)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(t)

\displaystyle=C_{0}\left(\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{R_{f}}(t-r)\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(r)\mathrm{d}r+\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}}(t)\right),\text{ with }\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(0)=C_{0}(1+6\gamma).

One may check using a Laplace transform argument that
these systems admit a unique solution
\Phi_{R_{\theta}},\Phi_{R_{f}},\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}},\Phi_{C_{\theta}}:[0,T]\to[0,\infty)
, where all four envelope functions are nonnegative and increasing;
we refer to
[
30
, Lemma 3.1]
for details of such an argument.

Definition 3.1
.

Fix
T>0
and a constant
C_{0}>0
(which also defines
\Phi_{R_{\theta}},\Phi_{R_{f}},\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}},\Phi_{C_{\theta}}
).
Given a finite subset
D=\{d_{1},\ldots,d_{m}\}\subset[0,T]
, we call

[0,d_{1}),[d_{1},d_{2}),\ldots,[d_{m-1},d_{m}),[d_{m},T]

the maximal intervals of
[0,T]\setminus D
.

Let
\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\equiv\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
be the space of all
tuples
(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)
such that there exists a finite set

D\subset[0,T]
for which:

(1)

C_{f}\equiv\{C_{f}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}
is a symmetric covariance kernel
on
[0,T]\otimes\mathbb{R}^{k}
, i.e.
C_{f}^{t,s}=(C_{f}^{s,t})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
for all
t,s\in[0,T]
,
and for any finite subset
T_{0}\subset[0,T]
,
the matrix
(C_{f}^{t,s})_{t,s\in T_{0}}\in\mathbb{R}^{k|T_{0}|\times k|T_{0}|}
is
symmetric positive-semidefinite. Furthermore, we have,

C_{f}^{0,0}=0,\qquad\operatorname{Tr}C_{f}^{t,t}\leq C_{0}\text{ for all }0\leq t\leq T,

and for each maximal interval
I
of
[0,T]\setminus D
,

(60)

\operatorname{Tr}[C_{f}^{t,t}-C_{f}^{t,s}-C_{f}^{s,t}+C_{f}^{s,s}]\leq C_{0}|t-s|\text{ for all }t,s\in I.

(2)

R_{f}\equiv\{R_{f}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
is a family of bounded linear operators

R_{f}^{t}:L^{4}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{k})\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
.
Fixing any process
x\in L^{4}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
, the map

t\mapsto R_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})
is Borel-measurable, and

(61)

\|R_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})\|^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{R_{f}}(t-r)\|x^{r}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\text{ for all }t\in[0,T].

(3)

R_{f}^{*}\equiv\{R_{f}^{t,*}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
is a Borel-measurable
process of matrices in
\mathbb{R}^{k\times k^{*}}
, where

\|R_{f}^{t,*}\|^{2}\leq\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}}(t)\text{ for all }t\in[0,T].

(4)

\Gamma\equiv\{\Gamma^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
is a Borel-measurable process
of matrices in
\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
, where

\|\Gamma_{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq M_{f}\text{ for all }t\in[0,T].

Let
{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
be the space of
all tuples
(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})
such that there exists a finite set
D\subset[0,T]

for which:

(1)

C_{\theta}\equiv\{C_{\theta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]\cup\{*\}}
is a symmetric
covariance kernel on
([0,T]\otimes\mathbb{R}^{k})\times\mathbb{R}^{k^{*}}
, i.e.
C_{\theta}^{t,s}=(C_{\theta}^{s,t})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
,

C_{\theta}^{t,*}=(C_{\theta}^{*,t})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k^{*}}
, and

C_{\theta}^{*,*}\in\mathbb{R}^{k^{*}\times k^{*}}
for all
t,s\in[0,T]
, and
for any finite subset
T_{0}\subset[0,T]
,
the matrix
(C_{\theta}^{t,s})_{t,s\in T_{0}\cup\{*\}}\in\mathbb{R}^{(k|T_{0}|+k^{*})\times(k|T_{0}|+k^{*})}
is symmetric positive-semidefinite. Furthermore,
we have

C_{\theta}^{*,*}=\mathbb{E}[\theta^{*}\theta^{*\top}],\quad C_{\theta}^{0,0}=\mathbb{E}[\theta^{0}\theta^{0\top}],\quad C_{\theta}^{0,*}=\mathbb{E}[\theta^{0}\theta^{*\top}],

\operatorname{Tr}C_{\theta}^{t,t}\leq\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(t)\text{ for all }0\leq t\leq T,

and for each maximal interval
I
of
[0,T]\setminus D
,

(62)

\operatorname{Tr}[C_{\theta}^{t,t}-C_{\theta}^{t,s}-C_{\theta}^{s,t}+C_{\theta}^{s,s}]\leq(\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(T)+C_{0}^{2})|t-s|\text{ for all }t,s\in I.

(2)

R_{\theta}\equiv\{R_{\theta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}
is a Borel-measurable
process of matrices in
\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
, satisfying

R_{\theta}^{t,s}=0\text{ for all $s>t$},\qquad\|R_{\theta}^{t,s}\|^{2}\leq\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-s)\text{ for all }s\leq t,

and for each maximal interval
I
of
[0,T]\setminus D
,

\|R_{\theta}^{t^{\prime},s}-R_{\theta}^{t,s}\|^{2}\leq\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(T)|t^{\prime}-t|\text{ for all }s\in[0,T]\text{ and }t,t^{\prime}\in I\text{ with }t,t^{\prime}\geq s.

We set

\mathcal{S}\equiv\mathcal{S}(T,C_{0})=\{(C_{\theta},R_{\theta},C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma):(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})\in\mathcal{S}_{\theta},\,(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)\in\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\}.

We denote by

\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{cont}},\mathcal{S}_{\xi}^{\mathrm{cont}},\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\mathrm{cont}}

these spaces where the above conditions hold with
D=\emptyset
,
i.e. where the continuity conditions for

C_{f},C_{\theta},R_{\theta}
hold on the whole interval
[0,T]
.

Note that the continuity conditions (
60
) and
(
62
) for
C_{f}
and
C_{\theta}

ensure (via Kolmogorov’s continuity theorem)
the existence of Gaussian processes
\{u^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}\sim\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{f})

and
(\{w^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]},w^{*})\sim\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{\theta})
with càdlàg sample
paths (having discontinuities only in
D
),
as claimed in the constructions of Section
2.3
.

3.2.
Existence and uniqueness of stochastic processes

We first establish
2.5
(a), showing
the existence and uniqueness of solutions to the
stochastic integro-differential equations defined in
(
13
–
17
), given any

(C_{\theta},R_{\theta},C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)\in\mathcal{S}
. (Here
and throughout, a solution
\{Y^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
refers to a process that
satisfies the desired equation a.s. at each
t\in[0,T]
.
The solution is unique if any other solution
\widetilde{Y}
is a modification of
Y
, i.e.
Y^{t}=\widetilde{Y}^{t}
a.s. for each
t\in[0,T]
, implying in the case where
Y
and
\widetilde{Y}
are both càdlàg that they have equal sample paths a.s.)

The following general lemma shows the well-posedness of a class of
Volterra-type SDEs, which we will use to show existence and uniqueness
of the specific processes
\xi^{t}
,
r_{f}^{t,*}
, and
r_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}\}_{t\geq 0},\mathbb{P})
be the filtered probability space
of Theorem
2.5
(a), where

\{z^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
is either the Poisson process
(
12
) or the Gaussian diffusion process (
24
).
Fix any
m\geq 1
, let
\{F^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
and
\{G^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be càdlàg
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-adapted
processes in
\mathbb{R}^{m\times k}
and
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, and let

H:\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{k^{*}}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
be a function that is
uniformly Lipschitz-continuous in its first argument. Suppose,
for some deterministic constants
M>0
and
\iota>0
that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|F^{t}\|\leq M\text{ a.s.},\qquad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\|G^{t}\|^{2+\iota}<\infty,\qquad\mathbb{E}\|H(0,w^{*},\varepsilon)\|^{2+\iota}<\infty.

Then for any
R_{\theta}\equiv\{R_{\theta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}

satisfying the conditions of Definition
3.1
,
there exists a unique
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-adapted and càdlàg solution

\{Y^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
to

(63)

Y^{t}=F^{t}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta}^{t,r}H(Y^{r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}+G^{t}.

Proof.

Let
L^{2+\iota,\infty}(\Omega\times[0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
be the
space of jointly measurable and
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-adapted processes
\{Y^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
(identified up to modifications)
for which

\|Y\|_{L^{2+\iota,\infty}}:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\mathbb{E}\|Y^{t}\|^{2+\iota})^{\frac{1}{2+\iota}}<\infty
.
Initialize
Y^{(0)}=G\in L^{2+\iota}(\Omega\times[0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
, and
suppose inductively we have constructed a càdlàg process

Y^{(n)}\in L^{2+\iota}(\Omega\times[0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
.
Consider the Volterra-type Itô integral

I^{(n),t}=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta}^{t,r}H(Y^{(n),r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r},

which defines a jointly measurable and adapted process
\{I^{(n),t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

by
[
9
, Lemma 2.A]
in the setting of Gaussian
\{z^{t}\}
or
by a pathwise construction for each
\omega\in\Omega
in the setting of
Poisson
\{z^{t}\}
. Recall the maximal intervals of
[0,T]\setminus D

in Definition
3.1
on which
t\in[r,T]\mapsto R_{\theta}^{t,r}

is uniformly continuous. For Poisson

\{z^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, it is clear that this continuity implies
I^{(n),t}

is càdlàg over each such maximal interval, and hence also over

[0,T]
, with discontinuities at the jumps of
\{z^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
and the
points of
D
. For Gaussian
\{z^{t}\}
, note that
for any
s<t
belonging to the same maximal interval of
[0,T]\setminus D
,
we have by the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality
(c.f. Lemma
A.1
) and Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|I^{(n),t}-I^{(n),s}\|^{2+\iota}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}(R_{\theta}^{t,r}-R_{\theta}^{s,r})H(Y^{(n),r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2+\iota}+\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta}^{t,r}H(Y^{(n),r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2+\iota}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{s}\|(R_{\theta}^{t,r}-R_{\theta}^{s,r})H(Y^{(n),r},w^{*},\varepsilon)\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{1+\iota/2}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\|R_{\theta}^{t,r}H(Y^{(n),r},w^{*},\varepsilon)\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{1+\iota/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{s}\mathbb{E}\|(R_{\theta}^{t,r}-R_{\theta}^{s,r})H(Y^{(n),r},w^{*},\varepsilon)\|^{2+\iota}\mathrm{d}r+|t-s|^{\iota/2}\int_{s}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|R_{\theta}^{t,r}H(Y^{(n),r},w^{*},\varepsilon)\|^{2+\iota}\mathrm{d}r.

Then, by the boundedness and continuity conditions for
R_{\theta}
in Definition

3.1
and the Lipschitz continuity of
H(\cdot)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|I^{(n),t}-I^{(n),s}\|^{2+\iota}

\displaystyle\lesssim|t-s|^{2+\iota}\left(\mathbb{E}\|H(0,w^{*},\varepsilon)\|^{2+\iota}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\|Y^{(n),r}\|^{2+\iota}\mathrm{d}r\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+|t-s|^{\iota/2}\left(|t-s|\,\mathbb{E}\|H(0,w^{*},\varepsilon)\|^{2+\iota}+\int_{s}^{t}\|Y^{(n),r}\|^{2+\iota}\mathrm{d}r\right)

(64)

\displaystyle\lesssim|t-s|^{2+\iota}+|t-s|^{1+\iota/2}.

Kolmogorov’s continuity theorem then implies that
\{I^{(n),t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

has a modification which is uniformly continuous on each maximal interval of

[0,T]\setminus D
and hence càdlàg on
[0,T]
.

Then defining

(65)

Y^{(n+1),t}=F^{t}I^{(n),t}+G^{t}=F^{t}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta}^{t,r}H(Y^{(n),r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}+G^{t},

Y^{(n+1)}
is adapted and càdlàg.
Applying the boundedness of
\{F^{t}\}
, the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy type
inequality of Lemma
A.1
in both the Poisson and Gaussian
settings, and Hölder’s inequality as above,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|Y^{(n+1),t}\|^{2+\iota}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta}^{t,r}H(Y^{(n),r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2+\iota}+\mathbb{E}\|G^{t}\|^{2+\iota}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\|H(0,w^{*},\varepsilon)\|^{2+\iota}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\|Y^{(n),r}\|^{2+\iota}\mathrm{d}r+\mathbb{E}\|G^{t}\|^{2+\iota}.

Thus
Y^{(n+1)}\in L^{2+\iota,\infty}(\Omega\times[0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
, so

Y^{(n+1)}
is inductively well-defined for each
n\geq 0
.

Now consider the difference
Y^{(n),t}-Y^{(n-1),t}
,
and set
\phi_{n}(t)=\mathbb{E}\|Y^{(n),t}-Y^{(n-1),t}\|^{2+\iota}
.
Then again by Lemma
A.1
and similar arguments as above,

\displaystyle\phi_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta}^{t,r}H(Y^{(0),r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2+\iota}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\|H(0,w^{*},\varepsilon)\|^{2+\iota}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\|G^{r}\|^{2+\iota}\mathrm{d}r,

\displaystyle\phi_{n+1}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta}^{t,r}\Big(H(Y^{(n),r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)-H(Y^{(n-1),r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\Big)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2+\iota}

(66)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|Y^{(n),r}-Y^{(n-1),r}\|^{2+\iota}\mathrm{d}r=\int_{0}^{t}\phi_{n}(r)\mathrm{d}r.

I.e., there exists a constant
C>0
(depending on
M,T,\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(T),M_{\eta},H
,
and the law of
(w^{*},\varepsilon)
) for which

\phi_{1}(t)\leq C
and
\phi_{n+1}(t)\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\phi_{n}(r)\mathrm{d}r
.
Then
\phi_{n}(t)\leq C^{n}t^{n-1}/(n-1)!
, implying that
\{Y^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 0}

is Cauchy in
L^{2+\iota,\infty}(\Omega\times[0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
and thus has a
limit
Y\in L^{2+\iota,\infty}(\Omega\times[0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\|Y^{(n),t}-Y^{t}\|^{2+\iota}=0
. Defining the process

I^{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta}^{t,r}H(Y^{r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}

as above, we then have
\mathbb{E}\|Y^{t}-(F^{t}I^{t}+G^{t})\|^{2+\iota}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\|Y^{(n+1),t}-(F^{t}I^{(n),t}+G^{t})\|^{2+\iota}=0
for each
t\in[0,T]
,
so
\{Y^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
solves (
63
). The
same argument as (
64
) implies that

\{I^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
and
\{Y^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
have càdlàg
modifications.

For uniqueness, let
\{\widetilde{Y}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be any other adapted
and càdlàg solution
to (
63
). Fix any
K>0
, define
the stopping time
\tau=\inf\{t\in[0,T]:\widetilde{Y}^{t}\geq K\}
,
and consider the stopped process

(\widetilde{Y}^{\tau})^{t}=\widetilde{Y}^{\tau\wedge t}
. Then
by Lemma
A.1
and a similar argument as above,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|(\widetilde{Y}^{\tau})^{t}\|^{2+\iota}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|F^{t\wedge\tau}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{1}\{r\leq\tau\}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta}^{t,r}H(\widetilde{Y}^{r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}+G^{t\wedge\tau}\right\|^{2+\iota}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{1}\{r\leq\tau\}\widetilde{Y}^{r-}\|^{2+\iota}\mathrm{d}r\lesssim 1+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|(\widetilde{Y}^{\tau})^{r}\|^{2+\iota}\mathrm{d}r.

Note that the constant underlying
\lesssim
is independent of
K
, so Grönwall’s
inequality implies that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\|(\widetilde{Y}^{\tau})^{t}\|^{2+\iota}
is uniformly
bounded independently of
K
. Taking
K\to\infty

shows that
\widetilde{Y}\in L^{2+\iota,\infty}(\Omega\times[0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
.
Then defining
\phi(t)=\mathbb{E}\|Y^{t}-\widetilde{Y}^{t}\|^{2+\iota}
,
the same argument as (
66
) shows

\phi(t)\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\phi(s)\mathrm{d}s
, so
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\phi(t)=0
by Grönwall’s inequality, and
\widetilde{Y}
is a modification of
Y
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.5
(a).

Let
(C_{\theta},R_{\theta},C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)\in\mathcal{S}
be given, and let

(\theta,\theta^{*})
,
\varepsilon
,
\{u^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
,
(\{w^{t}\}_{t\geq[0,T]},w^{*})
, and
\{z^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
in the filtered space

(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}\}_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
also be given. The bound for

C_{f}
in Definition
3.1
implies that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\|w^{t}\|^{2+\iota}<\infty
for any
\iota>0
.
Then there exists a unique
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-adapted and càdlàg
solution
\{\xi^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
to (
14
) by

3.2
. Given this solution and any
deterministic process
x\in L^{4}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
, define next

G^{t}={-}\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta}^{t,r}\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)x^{r}\mathrm{d}z^{r}.

This process
\{G^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
is càdlàg and satisfies
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|G^{t}\|^{2+\iota}<\infty
for
sufficiently small
\iota>0
, by
the same arguments as in
3.2
.
Then there exist unique
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-adapted and càdlàg solutions

\{r_{f}^{t,*}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
and

\{r_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})\}_{t\in[0,T]}
to
(
16
–
17
)
also by
3.2
.

For (
13
), set
\tilde{\theta}^{t}=\theta^{t}-\sqrt{\gamma}\,u^{t}
. Note that since
C_{f}^{0,0}=0
, we have

u^{0}=0
and
\tilde{\theta}^{0}=\theta^{0}
. Then

\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
solves
(
13
) if and only if

\{\tilde{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
solves the differential equation

(67)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\tilde{\theta}^{t}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{-\bar{\eta}^{t}\Big({\gamma}\Gamma^{t}(\tilde{\theta}^{t}+\sqrt{\gamma}\,u^{t})+g(\tilde{\theta}^{t}+\sqrt{\gamma}\,u^{t})+{\gamma}R_{f}^{t}(\tilde{\theta}^{[t]})+{\gamma^{3/2}}R_{f}^{t}(u^{[t]})+{\gamma}R_{f}^{t,*}\theta^{*}\Big)}_{:=F(t,\tilde{\theta}^{[t]})},\quad\tilde{\theta}^{0}=\theta^{0}.

The bound (
61
) implies that

R_{f}^{t}
restricts to an operator on
C([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{k})\subset L^{4}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{k})
that
is Lipschitz in the sup-norm
\|x\|_{\infty}=\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|x^{s}\|_{2}
,
uniformly over
t\in[0,T]
. Then the function
(t,x)\mapsto F(t,x^{[t]})

on the right side above is measurable
in
t
and uniformly Lipschitz in
x\in C([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
, implying
(c.f.
[
38
, Theorems 2.1, 2.3]
)
the existence of a unique continuous solution to (
67
)
for any realization of
(\theta^{0},\theta^{*})
and

\{u^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
. This defines, pathwise for each
\omega\in\Omega
,
a unique
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-adapted and càdlàg solution

\theta^{t}=\tilde{\theta}^{t}+\sqrt{\gamma}\,u^{t}
to (
13
), with discontinuities at the jumps of
\{u^{t}\}
.
Given this solution
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, the same argument shows that
for each fixed
s\in[0,T]
, there is a unique continuous solution

\{r_{\theta}^{t,s}\}_{t\in[s,T]}
to

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}r_{\theta}^{t,s}={-}\bar{\eta}^{t}[(\gamma\Gamma^{t}+\mathrm{D}g(\theta^{t})r_{\theta}^{t,s}+\gamma R_{f}^{t}(r_{\theta}^{[t],s})],\quad r_{\theta}^{s,s}=\mathrm{Id}_{k},

which is equivalent to (
15
).
∎

3.3.
Closure of the mappings

Given
(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})\in\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
, consider the processes (
14
),
(
16
), and (
17
)
defined on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0},\mathbb{P})
and let

\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})=(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)
be the mapping defined by
(
20
–
23
). Similarly, given

(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)\in\mathcal{S}_{\xi}
, consider the processes
(
13
) and (
15
) and let

\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)=(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})
be the mapping defined by
(
18
–
19
).

In this section, we check that for a sufficiently large choice of constant

C_{0}>0
defining
\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\xi}
,

\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}
maps
{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}
into

\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{cont}}_{\xi}\subset\mathcal{S}_{\xi}
, and

\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}
maps
\mathcal{S}_{\xi}
into
{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Fix any
T>0
. Then for any constant
C_{0}>0
large enough (depending on
T
,

M_{f}
,
M_{g}
,
M_{\eta}
,
\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{0}\|^{2}
,
\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}
, and

\|g(0)\|^{2}
),

\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}
maps
\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
into
{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
, and

\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{cont}}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
into

{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cont}}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
.

Proof.

Assume
(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)\in S_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
, and denote

(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})=\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)
.

We note that by its definition in (
18
),
the kernel

\{C_{\theta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]\cup\{*\}}
is positive-semidefinite
with
C_{\theta}^{*,*},C_{\theta}^{0,*},C_{\theta}^{0,0}
as specified in Definition

3.1
. Recall from (
13
) that

(68)

\displaystyle\theta^{t}

\displaystyle=\theta^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}\Big({\gamma}\Gamma^{r}\theta^{r}+g(\theta^{r})+{\gamma}R_{f}^{r}(\theta^{[r]})+{\gamma}R_{f}^{r,*}\theta^{*}\Big)\mathrm{d}r+\sqrt{\gamma}\,u^{t}.

Applying Cauchy-Schwarz and the bound
(\int_{0}^{t}f(s)\mathrm{d}s)^{2}\leq t\int_{0}^{t}f(s)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq T\int_{0}^{t}f(s)^{2}\mathrm{d}s
, we have
\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{t}\|^{2}\leq 6((\mathrm{I})+(\mathrm{II})+(\mathrm{III})+(\mathrm{IV})+(\mathrm{V})+(\mathrm{VI}))
where

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{0}\|^{2}

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}\gamma\Gamma^{r}\theta^{r}\mathrm{d}r\right\|^{2}\leq\gamma^{2}M_{f}^{2}M_{\eta}^{2}T\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{r}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{III})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}g(\theta^{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\|^{2}\leq M_{\eta}^{2}T\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|g(\theta^{r})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\leq 2M_{g}^{2}M_{\eta}^{2}T\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{r}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r+2M_{\eta}^{2}T^{2}\|g(0)\|^{2},\,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{IV})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}\gamma R_{f}^{r}(\theta^{[r]})\mathrm{d}r\right\|^{2}\leq\gamma^{2}M_{\eta}^{2}T\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|R_{f}^{r}(\theta^{[r]})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\leq}\gamma^{2}M_{\eta}^{2}T\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{r^{\prime}}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}r,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{V})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}\gamma R_{f}^{r,*}\theta^{*}\mathrm{d}r\right\|^{2}\leq\gamma^{2}M_{\eta}^{2}T\,\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}}(r)\mathrm{d}r,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{VI})

\displaystyle=\gamma\,\mathbb{E}\|u^{t}\|^{2}=\gamma\operatorname{Tr}C_{f}^{t,t}\leq\gamma C_{0}.

Here, we have used the property (
61
) of
R_{f}^{t}(\cdot)

to bound
(*)
.
Collecting these bounds shows, for any constant
C_{0}>0
large enough,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left(1+6\gamma+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{r}\|^{2}+\int_{0}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{r^{\prime}}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}}(r)\right)\mathrm{d}r\right).

The definition of
\Phi_{C_{\theta}}
in (
59
) implies

(69)

\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(t)=C_{0}\left(1+6\gamma+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(r)+\int_{0}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}}(r)\right)\mathrm{d}r\right),

so comparing with the above shows

\operatorname{Tr}C_{\theta}^{t,t}=\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{t}\|^{2}\leq\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(t).

Next, let
D
be the discontinuity set of
(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)\in\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
.
For each maximal interval
I
of
[0,T]\setminus D

and any
s,t\in I
, by an analogous application of Cauchy-Schwarz
and this bound
\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{t}\|^{2}\leq\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(t)
, for any constant

C_{0}>0
large enough,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}[C_{\theta}^{t,t}-C_{\theta}^{t,s}-C_{\theta}^{s,t}+C_{\theta}^{s,s}]=\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{t}-\theta^{s}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{s}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}\Big({\gamma}\Gamma^{r}\theta^{r}+g(\theta^{r})+{\gamma}R_{f}^{r}(\theta^{[r]})+{\gamma}R_{f}^{r,*}\theta^{*}\Big)\mathrm{d}r+\sqrt{\gamma}(u^{t}-u^{s})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|t-s|\left(1+\int_{s}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{r}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r+\int_{s}^{t}\int_{0}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{r^{\prime}}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\mathrm{d}r+\int_{s}^{t}\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}}(r)\mathrm{d}r\right)+C_{0}\mathbb{E}\|u^{t}-u^{s}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|t-s|\left(1+\int_{0}^{T}\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(r)\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{T}\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}}(r)\mathrm{d}r\right)+C_{0}\mathbb{E}\|u^{t}-u^{s}\|^{2}

(70)

\displaystyle\leq(\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(T)+C_{0}^{2})|t-s|

where the last inequality applies

\mathbb{E}\|u^{t}-u^{s}\|^{2}=\operatorname{Tr}[C_{f}^{t,t}-C_{f}^{t,s}-C_{f}^{s,t}+C_{f}^{s,s}]\leq C_{0}|t-s|
by (
60
), and the form of

\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(T)
in (
69
).
This checks all conditions of Definition
3.1
for
C_{\theta}
.

For
R_{\theta}
, recall from (
15
) that

(71)

\displaystyle r_{\theta}^{t,s}=\mathrm{Id}-\int_{s}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}\bigg[\Big({\gamma}\Gamma^{r}+\mathrm{D}g(\theta^{r})\Big)r_{\theta}^{r,s}+{\gamma}R_{f}^{r}\Big(r_{\theta}^{[r],s}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}r\text{ for }t\geq s.

Then
\|r_{\theta}^{t,s}\|^{2}\leq 4((\mathrm{I})+(\mathrm{II})+(\mathrm{III})+(\mathrm{IV}))
where

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})

\displaystyle=\|\mathrm{Id}\|^{2}=k

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{s}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}\gamma\Gamma^{r}r_{\theta}^{r,s}\mathrm{d}r\right\|^{2}\leq\gamma^{2}M_{f}^{2}M_{\eta}^{2}T\int_{s}^{t}\|r_{\theta}^{r,s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{III})

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{s}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}\mathrm{D}g(\theta^{r})r_{\theta}^{r,s}\mathrm{d}r\right\|^{2}\leq M_{g}^{2}M_{\eta}^{2}T\int_{s}^{t}\|r_{\theta}^{r,s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{IV})

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{s}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}\gamma R_{f}^{r}\Big(r_{\theta}^{[r],s}\Big)\mathrm{d}r\right\|^{2}\leq\gamma^{2}M_{\eta}^{2}T\int_{s}^{t}\Big\|R_{f}^{r}\Big(r_{\theta}^{[r],s}\Big)\Big\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{2}M_{\eta}^{2}T\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\|r_{\theta}^{r^{\prime},s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\mathrm{d}r,

the last inequality again using (
61
) and the fact that

r_{\theta}^{r^{\prime},s}=0
for
r^{\prime}<s
. Collecting these bounds shows

\|r_{\theta}^{t,s}\|^{2}\leq C_{0}\left(1+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\|r_{\theta}^{r,s}\|^{2}+\int_{s}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\|r_{\theta}^{r^{\prime},s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}r\right)

for any constant
C_{0}>0
large enough.
The definition of
\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t)
in (
56
) implies

(72)

\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-s)=C_{0}\left(1+\int_{0}^{t-s}\left(\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(r)+\int_{0}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}r\right),

so comparing with the above shows

\|R_{\theta}^{t,s}\|^{2}=\|\mathbb{E}r_{\theta}^{t,s}\|^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta}^{t,s}\|^{2}\leq\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-s).

Then, applying this bound

\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta}^{t,s}\|^{2}\leq\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-s)
,
we have similarly for any
C_{0}>0
large enough and any
t\geq t^{\prime}\geq s
that

\displaystyle\|R_{\theta}^{t,s}-R_{\theta}^{t^{\prime},s}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta}^{t,s}-r_{\theta}^{t^{\prime},s}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|t-t^{\prime}|\left(\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta}^{r,s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r+\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{s}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta}^{r^{\prime},s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\mathrm{d}r\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|t-t^{\prime}|\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(r-s)\mathrm{d}r+\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(r^{\prime}-s)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\mathrm{d}r\right)

(73)

\displaystyle\leq|t-t^{\prime}|\cdot\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-s)\leq|t-t^{\prime}|\cdot\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(T),

where the last two inequalities apply (
72
) and
monotonicity of
\Phi_{R_{\theta}}
.
This checks all conditions of Definition
3.1
for
R_{\theta}
, so

(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
.

We note that (
73
) holds for all
t,t^{\prime},s\in[0,T]
with

t\geq t^{\prime}\geq s
, irrespective of the discontinuity set

D
for
(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)\in\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
.
If
(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{cont}}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
, then
(
70
) also holds for all
t,s\in[0,T]
, implying by the
above arguments that the conditions of Definition
3.1
for

(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})
hold with
D=\emptyset
.
Thus also
(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}^{\mathrm{cont}}(T,C_{0})
.
∎

Lemma 3.4
.

Fix any
T>0
. Then for any constant
C_{0}>0
large enough (depending on
T
,

M_{f}
, and
M_{\eta}
),
\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}
maps
{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
into

\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{cont}}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
.

Proof.

In both the Poisson and Gaussian settings,

\{z^{t}-\bar{\kappa}t\}_{t\in[0,T]}
is a
martingale whose predictable quadratic variation is given by

\langle z^{t}-\bar{\kappa}t\rangle=\bar{\kappa}t
. Then for any
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-predictable square-integrable
process
\{X^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}X^{r}\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}X^{r}\bar{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}r\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\,\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}X^{r}(\mathrm{d}z^{r}-\bar{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}r)\right\|_{2}^{2}

(74)

\displaystyle=2\,\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}X^{r}\bar{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}r\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|X^{r}\|_{2}^{2}\,\bar{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq 2(t+1)\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|X^{r}\|_{2}^{2}\,\bar{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}r.

Assume
(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
, and denote

(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)=\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})
.
By its definition in (
20
),

C_{f}
is a positive-semidefinite kernel with
C_{f}^{0,0}=0
.
Applying (
74
), for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\operatorname{Tr}C_{f}^{t,t}=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\xi^{r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2}\leq 2(t+1)\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left\|\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\xi^{r},w^{*},\varepsilon)\right\|^{2}\bar{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C_{0}

for a large enough constant
C_{0}>0
. Similarly, for all
t,s\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}[C_{f}^{t,t}-C_{f}^{t,s}-C_{f}^{s,t}+C_{f}^{s,s}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\xi^{r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2}\leq C_{0}|t-s|

for large enough
C_{0}>0
.
This checks all conditions of Definition
3.1
for
C_{f}
.

For
R_{f}
, given any
x\in L^{4}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
it is clear from the definition of
r_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})

in (
17
) that

t\mapsto R_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})=\mathbb{E}r_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})
is Borel-measurable.
By (
17
) and (
74
),
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|r_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq M_{f}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta}^{t,r}\Big(r_{f}^{r-}(x^{[r]})+\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)x^{r-}\Big)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2}

(75)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-r)\Big(\mathbb{E}\|r_{f}^{r}(x^{[r]})\|^{2}+\|x^{r}\|^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}r

for large enough
C_{0}>0
. From the definition of
\Phi_{R_{f}}
in (
57
), we have

(76)

\Phi_{R_{f}}(t-r)=C_{0}\left(\int_{r}^{t}\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-r^{\prime})\Phi_{R_{f}}(r^{\prime}-r)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-r)\right).

Integrating both sides of the equation with respect to
{\|x^{r}\|^{2}}
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{R_{f}}(t-r)\|x^{r}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=C_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\left({\int_{r}^{t}\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-r^{\prime})\Phi_{R_{f}}(r^{\prime}-r)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-r)}\right)\|x^{r}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=C_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-r)\left({\int_{0}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\|x^{r^{\prime}}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{r^{\prime}}+\|x^{r}\|^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}r.

Comparing with (
75
), this shows that

(77)

\|R_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})\|^{2}=\|\mathbb{E}r_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})\|^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\|r_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})\|^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{R_{f}}(t-r)\|x^{r}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r.

Further bounding

(\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{R_{f}}(t-r)\|x^{r}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r)^{2}\leq t\Phi_{R_{f}}(t)^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|x^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r
, this verifies that

R_{f}^{t}
is a bounded linear operator from
L^{4}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{k})
to
\mathbb{R}^{k}
. This
checks all conditions of Definition
3.1
for
R_{f}
.

For
R_{f}^{*}
, we have similarly

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|r_{f}^{t,*}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left\|\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta}^{t,r}r_{f}^{r-,*}\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2}+2\mathbb{E}\|\mathrm{D}_{w^{*}}f(\xi^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left(1+\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-r)\mathbb{E}\|r_{f}^{r,*}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\right)

for large enough
C_{0}>0
. Comparing with the definition of
\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}}
in
(
58
) shows

\|R_{f}^{t,*}\|^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\|r_{f}^{t,*}\|^{2}\leq\Phi_{R_{f}^{*}}(t)
. Finally,
the measurability of
t\mapsto\Gamma^{t}
and bound

\|\Gamma_{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq M_{f}
follow directly from the definition of
\Gamma^{t}

in (
23
), and (
55
).
Thus
(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)\in\mathcal{S}_{\xi}^{\mathrm{cont}}(T,C_{0})
.
∎

3.4.
Contractivity of the mappings

We now endow
\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\equiv\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\equiv\mathcal{S}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
with metrics, under which we will show that

\mathcal{T}\equiv\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}\circ\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}
is contractive.

Fix a constant
\lambda>0
(which we will also take large enough, depending
on
T,C_{0}
). For covariance kernels
C_{f,1},C_{f,2}
on
[0,T]\otimes\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2}
on
([0,T]\otimes\mathbb{R}^{k})\times\mathbb{R}^{k^{*}}

satisfying the conditions of Definition
3.1
, define

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})=\inf_{(u_{1},u_{2})\sim\gamma\in\Gamma(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\big(\mathbb{E}{\|u^{t}_{1}-u^{t}_{2}\|^{4}}\big)^{1/4},

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})=\inf_{(w_{1},w_{2})\sim\gamma\in\Gamma(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\big(\mathbb{E}\|w^{t}_{1}-w^{t}_{2}\|^{4}\big)^{1/4},

where
\Gamma(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})
denotes the set of all couplings between the
Gaussian process laws
\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{f,1})
and
\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{f,2})
, and similarly for

\Gamma(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})
. Note that under the constraints and
continuity conditions for
C_{f},C_{\theta}
in Definition
3.1
, if

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})=0
, then

C_{\theta,1}^{t,s}=C_{\theta,2}^{t,s}
pointwise for all
t,s\in[0,T]\cup\{*\}
, and similarly for
C_{f}
.

For pairs of response kernels/operators satisfying Definition
3.1
, we
define also

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1},R_{f,2})

\displaystyle=\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\|R_{f,1}^{t}-R_{f,2}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1}^{*},R_{f,2}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\|R_{f,1}^{t,*}-R_{f,2}^{t,*}\|,

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})

\displaystyle=\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\|\Gamma_{1}-\Gamma_{2}\|,

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{\theta,1},R_{\theta,2})

\displaystyle=\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,s}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,s}\|,

where we recall that
\|R_{f}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
is the operator norm as a linear map
from
L^{4}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{k})
to
\mathbb{R}^{k}
.
Finally, for any
X_{i}=(C_{f,i},R_{f,i},R_{f,i}^{*},\Gamma_{i})\in\mathcal{S}_{\xi}

and
Y_{i}=(C_{\theta,i},R_{\theta,i})\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(X_{1},X_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1},R_{f,2})+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1}^{*},R_{f,2}^{*})+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}),

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y_{1},Y_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{\theta,1},R_{\theta,2}).

Throughout these arguments, we will fix
T,C_{0}>0
, and write

\lesssim
to mean inequality up to a constant depending on
T,C_{0}
.

We first study the contractivity properties for
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}
.

Lemma 3.5
.

Fix any
T,C_{0}>0
, suppose
X=(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)\in\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
, and let
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
and

\{r_{\theta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}
be defined
by (
13
) and (
15
).
Then there is a constant
C\equiv C(T,C_{0})>0
such that for all
t,s\in[0,T]
,

\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{t}\|^{4}\leq C,\qquad\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta}^{t,s}\|^{4}\leq C.

Proof.

By (
61
),

\|R_{f}^{r}(\theta^{[r]})\|^{4}\leq\left({\int_{0}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-s)\|\theta^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s}\right)^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{r}\|\theta^{s}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}s.

Then from the definition (
13
), we have

\displaystyle\|\theta^{t}\|^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta^{0}\|^{4}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(1+\|\theta^{r}\|^{4}+\|R_{f}^{r}(\theta^{[r]})\|^{4}+\|\theta^{*}\|^{4}\Big)\mathrm{d}r+\|u^{t}\|^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta^{0}\|^{4}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(1+\|\theta^{r}\|^{4}+\|\theta^{*}\|^{4}\Big)\mathrm{d}r+\|u^{t}\|^{4}.

Since
\mathbb{E}\|u^{t}\|^{4}\lesssim\left({\mathbb{E}\|u^{t}\|^{2}}\right)^{2}\lesssim\Phi_{C_{f}}(T)^{2}\lesssim 1
,
taking expectations and applying Grönwall’s inequality shows
the bound for
\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{t}\|^{4}
. A similar argument shows the bound
for
\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta}^{t,s}\|^{4}
.
∎

Lemma 3.6
.

Fix any
T,C_{0}>0
. For
i=1,2
,
suppose
X_{i}=(C_{f,i},R_{f,i},R_{f,i}^{*},\Gamma_{i})\in\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
,
and let
(C_{\theta,i},R_{\theta,i})=\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}(X_{i})
.
Then there exist constants
C\equiv C(T,C_{0})>0
and
\lambda_{0}\equiv\lambda_{0}(T,C_{0})>0
such that for any
\lambda>\lambda_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})

\displaystyle\leq C\,\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})

(79)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.45377pt+\frac{C}{\lambda}\left({\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1},R_{f,2})+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1}^{*},R_{f,2}^{*})}\right),

(80)

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{\theta,1},R_{\theta,2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(X_{1},X_{2}).

Proof.

By definition of
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\cdot,\cdot)
,
there exists a coupling
\{u_{1}^{t},u_{2}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
of
\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{f,1})
and

\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{f,2})
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}\big(\mathbb{E}\|u_{1}^{t}-u_{2}^{t}\|^{4}\big)^{1/4}\leq 2\,\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})^{4}.

For
i=1,2
, define
\theta_{i}^{t}
via (
13
) using

X_{i},\{u_{i}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
. Then

\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{t}_{1}-\theta^{t}_{2}\|^{4}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}((\mathrm{I})_{r}+(\mathrm{II})_{r})\mathrm{d}r+(\mathrm{III})
where

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})_{r}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|g(\theta_{1}^{r})-g(\theta_{2}^{r})+\gamma\Gamma_{1}^{r}(\theta_{1}^{r}-\theta_{2}^{r})+\gamma R_{f,1}^{r}(\theta_{1}^{[r]}-\theta_{2}^{[r]})\right\|^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\|\theta_{1}^{r}-\theta_{2}^{r}\|^{4}+\left(\int_{0}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{r^{\prime}}_{1}-\theta^{r^{\prime}}_{2}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{4\lambda r}e^{-4\lambda r}\mathbb{E}\|\theta_{1}^{r}-\theta_{2}^{r}\|^{4}+\int_{0}^{r}e^{4\lambda r^{\prime}}e^{-4\lambda r^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{r^{\prime}}_{1}-\theta^{r^{\prime}}_{2}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{4\lambda r}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\|\theta_{1}^{t}-\theta_{2}^{t}\|^{4},

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})_{r}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|(\Gamma_{1}^{r}-\Gamma_{2}^{r})\theta_{2}^{r}+(R_{f,1}^{r}-R_{f,2}^{r})\theta_{2}^{[r]}+(R_{f,1}^{r,*}-R_{f,2}^{r,*})\theta^{*}\right\|^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Gamma_{1}^{r}-\Gamma_{2}^{r}\|^{4}\mathbb{E}\|\theta_{2}^{r}\|^{4}+\|{R_{f,1}^{r}-R_{f,2}^{r}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{4}\mathbb{E}\|\theta_{2}^{[r]}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\|R_{f,1}^{r,*}-R_{f,2}^{r,*}\|^{4}\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{*}\|^{4}

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\lesssim}e^{4\lambda r}\Big(\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})^{4}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1},R_{f,2})^{4}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1}^{*},R_{f,2}^{*})^{4}\Big),

\displaystyle(\mathrm{III})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\|u_{1}^{t}-u_{2}^{t}\|^{4}\leq e^{4\lambda t}\cdot 2\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})^{4}.

For
(*)
, we have used
3.5
to bound

\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{t}_{2}\|^{4},\mathbb{E}\|\theta_{2}^{[r]}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\lesssim 1
, and applied the
definitions of
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\cdot,\cdot)
.
Combining these bounds, multiplying by
e^{-4\lambda t}
, and taking the
supremum of over
t\in[0,T]
, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\|\theta_{1}^{t}-\theta_{2}^{t}\|^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\|\theta_{1}^{t}-\theta_{2}^{t}\|^{4}\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}\int_{0}^{t}e^{4\lambda r}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\Big(\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})^{4}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1},R_{f,2})^{4}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1}^{*},R_{f,2}^{*})^{4}\Big)\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}\int_{0}^{t}e^{4\lambda r}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\|\theta_{1}^{t}-\theta_{2}^{t}\|^{4}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})^{4}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1},R_{f,2})^{4}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1}^{*},R_{f,2}^{*})^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})^{4}.

Thus for any
\lambda>\lambda_{0}(T,C_{0})
large enough, we may rearrange to get

(81)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\|\theta_{1}^{t}-\theta_{2}^{t}\|^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})^{4}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.45377pt+\frac{1}{\lambda}\left({\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})^{4}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1},R_{f,2})^{4}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1}^{*},R_{f,2}^{*})^{4}}\right).

Letting
\{w_{1}^{t},w_{2}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]\cup\{*\}}
be a jointly Gaussian process
with the same covariance as
\{\theta_{1}^{t},\theta_{2}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]\cup\{*\}}
, this gives a coupling of
C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2}
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}\mathbb{E}{\|w^{t}_{1}-w^{t}_{2}\|^{4}}\lesssim\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}(\mathbb{E}{\|w^{t}_{1}-w^{t}_{2}\|^{2}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}(\mathbb{E}{\|\theta^{t}_{1}-\theta^{t}_{2}\|^{2}})^{2}\lesssim\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}\mathbb{E}{\|\theta^{t}_{1}-\theta^{t}_{2}\|^{4}}.

Combining the above shows (
79
).

For the analysis of
R_{\theta}
, we have similarly
from (
15
) that

\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,1}^{t,s}-r_{\theta,2}^{t,s}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{s}^{t}((\mathrm{I})_{r}+(\mathrm{II})_{r})\mathrm{d}r
where

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})_{r}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big\|(\gamma\Gamma_{1}^{r}+\mathrm{D}g(\theta_{1}^{r}))(r_{\theta,1}^{r,s}-r_{\theta,2}^{r,s})+\gamma R_{f}^{r}(r_{\theta,1}^{[r],s}-r_{\theta,2}^{[r],s})\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,1}^{r,s}-r_{\theta,2}^{r,s}\|^{2}+\int_{s}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,1}^{r^{\prime},s}-r_{\theta,2}^{r^{\prime},s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{2\lambda r}\sup_{t\in[s,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,1}^{t,s}-r_{\theta,2}^{t,s}\|^{2},

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})_{r}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big\|\gamma(\Gamma^{t}_{1}-\Gamma^{t}_{2})r_{\theta,2}^{r,s}+(\mathrm{D}g(\theta^{r}_{1})-\mathrm{D}g(\theta^{r}_{2}))r_{\theta,2}^{r,s}+\gamma(R_{f,1}^{r}-R_{f,2}^{r})(r_{\theta,2}^{[r],s})\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Gamma_{1}^{r}-\Gamma_{2}^{r}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,2}^{r,s}\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}[\|\mathrm{D}g(\theta_{1})-\mathrm{D}g(\theta_{2})\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\|r_{\theta,2}^{r,s}\|^{2}]+\|R_{f,1}^{r}-R_{f,2}^{r}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,2}^{[r],s}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Gamma_{1}^{r}-\Gamma_{2}^{r}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,2}^{r,s}\|^{2}+(\mathbb{E}\|\theta_{1}^{r}-\theta_{2}^{r}\|^{4})^{1/2}(\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,2}^{r,s}\|^{4})^{1/2}+\|R_{f,1}^{r}-R_{f,2}^{r}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,2}^{[r],s}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{2\lambda r}\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}.

Here,
\|r_{\theta}^{[r],s}\|_{L^{4}}
denotes

(\int_{0}^{r}\|r_{\theta}^{r^{\prime},s}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime})^{1/4}
,
and the last step using again
3.5

to bound
\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,2}^{[r],s}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\lesssim 1
, together with the
bound
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}(\mathbb{E}\|\theta_{1}^{t}-\theta_{2}^{t}\|^{4})^{1/2}\lesssim\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}
implied by (
81
).
Multiplying by
e^{-\lambda t}
and taking the supremum over
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,1}^{t,s}-r_{\theta,2}^{t,s}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,1}^{t,s}-r_{\theta,2}^{t,s}\|^{2}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}\right)\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\int_{s}^{t}e^{\lambda r}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\|r_{\theta,1}^{t,s}-r_{\theta,2}^{t,s}\|^{2}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}\right),

and rearranging shows (
80
).
∎

We next derive the contraction properties for the transformation
\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}
.

Lemma 3.7
.

Fix any
T,C_{0}>0
.
Let
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}\}_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
be the filtered probability
space of Theorem
2.5
, and
let
\{z^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be either the Poisson process (
12
)
or Gaussian process (
24
).

For
i=1,2
, suppose
Y_{i}=(C_{\theta,i},R_{\theta,i})\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
, and let
\{w_{1}^{t},w_{2}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]\cup\{*\}}

be any coupling of
w_{1}\sim\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{\theta,1})

and
w_{2}\sim\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{\theta,2})
. Define
\xi_{i}^{t}
,

r_{f,i}^{t,*}
, and
r_{f,i}^{t}(x^{[t]})
,
via (
14
), (
16
), and
(
17
) using

Y_{i},\{w_{i}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
.
Then there exists a constant
C\equiv C(T,C_{0})>0
such that for all
t\in[0,T]
and any process
x\in L^{4}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
,

(82)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\xi^{t}_{1}-\xi^{t}_{2}\right\|^{4}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{4}+\mathbb{E}\|w_{1}^{t}-w_{2}^{t}\|^{4}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|r_{f,1}^{t}(x^{[t]})-r_{f,2}^{t}(x^{[t]})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{2}+(\mathbb{E}\|w_{1}^{t}-w_{2}^{t}\|^{4})^{1/2}+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|w_{1}^{r}-w_{2}^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{1/2}\right)

(83)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\times\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|x^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{1/2},

(84)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|r_{f,1}^{t,*}-r_{f,2}^{t,*}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{2}+(\mathbb{E}\|w_{1}^{t}-w_{2}^{t}\|^{4})^{1/2}\right).

Proof.

Applying the form of
\xi_{i}^{t}
, Lemma
A.1
, and
the boundedness and Lipschitz properties of
f
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\xi^{t}_{1}-\xi^{t}_{2}\right\|^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}\left({R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}f(\xi_{1}^{r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}f(\xi_{2}^{r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)}\right)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{4}+\mathbb{E}\left\|w^{t}_{1}-w^{t}_{2}\right\|^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}f(\xi_{1}^{r},w^{*},\varepsilon)-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}f(\xi_{2}^{r},w^{*},\varepsilon)\right\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r+\mathbb{E}\left\|w^{t}_{1}-w^{t}_{2}\right\|^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{4}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{r}-\xi_{2}^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r+\mathbb{E}\left\|w^{t}_{1}-w^{t}_{2}\right\|^{4}.

Then Grönwall’s inequality shows (
82
).

For (
83
), applying the form of
r_{f,i}^{t}(x^{[t]})

and the Lipschitz property of
f
, we have

\mathbb{E}\|r_{f,1}^{t}(x^{[t]})-r_{f,2}^{t}(x^{[t]})\|^{2}\lesssim(\mathrm{I})+(\mathrm{II})+(\mathrm{III})
,
where

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}\left({R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}(r_{f,1}^{r-}(x^{[r]})-r_{f,2}^{r-}(x^{[r]}))+\left({R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}}\right)r_{f,2}^{r-}(x^{[r]})}\right)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}\left({R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}(\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{r-}_{1},w^{*},\varepsilon)-\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{r-}_{2},w^{*},\varepsilon))+\left({R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}}\right)\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{r-}_{2},w^{*},\varepsilon)}\right)x^{r-}\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle(\mathrm{III})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\left({\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{t}_{1},w^{*},\varepsilon)-\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{t}_{2},w^{*},\varepsilon)}\right)\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\bigl(r_{f,2}^{r-}(x^{[r]})+\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{r-}_{2},w^{*},\varepsilon)x^{r-}\bigr)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2}.

Using (
74
) and (
77
),
the first term can be bounded as

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\left({\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-r)^{2}\mathbb{E}\big\|r_{f,1}^{r}(x^{[r]})-r_{f,2}^{r}(x^{[r]})\big\|^{2}+\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{2}\mathbb{E}\|r_{f,2}^{r}(x^{[r]})\|^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\big\|r_{f,1}^{r}(x^{[r]})-r_{f,2}^{r}(x^{[r]})\big\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{t}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{2}\int_{0}^{r}\Phi_{R_{f}}(r-r^{\prime})^{2}\|x^{r^{\prime}}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\big\|r_{f,1}^{r}(x^{[r]})-r_{f,2}^{r}(x^{[r]})\big\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r+\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{2}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\|x^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{1/2}.

Similarly, the second term is bounded as

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{r}-\xi_{2}^{r}\|^{2}\cdot\|x^{r}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r+\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|x^{r}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{r}-\xi_{2}^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\|x^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{1/2}+\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{2}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\|x^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{1/2}

The third term is bounded as

\displaystyle(\mathrm{III})

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[{\|\xi_{1}^{t}-\xi_{2}^{t}\|^{2}\cdot\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\bigl(r_{f,2}^{r-}(x^{[r]})+\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{r-}_{2},w^{*},\varepsilon)x^{r-}\bigr)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{t}-\xi_{2}^{t}\|^{4})^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\bigl(r_{f,2}^{r-}(x^{[r]})+\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{r-}_{2},w^{*},\varepsilon)x^{r-}\bigr)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{4}\right)^{1/2}.

Applying Lemma
A.1
to bound the second factor, we have

(\mathrm{III})\lesssim(\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{t}-\xi_{2}^{t}\|^{4})^{1/2}\cdot\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathbb{E}\|r_{f,2}^{r}(x^{[r]})\|^{4}+\|x^{r}\|^{4}}\right)\mathrm{d}r\right)^{1/2}.

Applying Lemma
A.1
again to the definition of

r_{f}^{t}(\cdot)
shows

\mathbb{E}\|r_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})\|^{4}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\left(\mathbb{E}\|r_{f}^{r}(x^{[r]})\|^{4}+\|x^{r}\|^{4}\right)\mathrm{d}r,

so Grönwall’s inequality implies

\mathbb{E}\|r_{f}^{t}(x^{[t]})\|^{4}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|x^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r
.
Therefore

\displaystyle(\mathrm{III})

\displaystyle\leq(\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{t}-\xi_{2}^{t}\|^{4})^{1/2}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|x^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{1/2}.

Combining the above bounds, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\|r_{f,1}^{t}(x^{[t]})-r_{f,2}^{t}(x^{[t]})\big\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\big\|r_{f,1}^{r}(x^{[r]})-r_{f,2}^{r}(x^{[r]})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\hskip 14.45377pt+\left(\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{2}+(\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{t}-\xi_{2}^{t}\|^{4})^{1/2}+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{r}-\xi_{2}^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{1/2}\right)\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|x^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{1/2}.

Applying again Grönwall’s inequality and (
82
) to bound

\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{r}-\xi_{2}^{r}\|^{4}
, we obtain (
83
).

Finally, from the form of
r_{f,i}^{t,*}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\|r_{f,1}^{t,*}-r_{f,2}^{t,*}\|^{2}\lesssim(\mathrm{I})+(\mathrm{II})+(\mathrm{III})
,
where

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}\left({R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}r_{f,1}^{r-,*}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}r_{f,2}^{r-,*}}\right)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2},

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\Big(\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{1}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)-\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{2}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)\Big)\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}r_{f,1}^{r-,*}\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2},

\displaystyle(\mathrm{III})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\|\mathrm{D}_{w^{*}}f(\xi_{1}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)-\mathrm{D}_{w^{*}}f(\xi_{2}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)\|^{2}.

By similar arguments as above, we may bound each term to obtain

\mathbb{E}\|r_{f,1}^{t,*}-r_{f,2}^{t,*}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|r_{f,1}^{r,*}-r_{f,2}^{r,*}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r+\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{2}+(\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{t}-\xi_{2}^{t}\|^{4})^{1/2}.

Applying Grönwall’s inequality and (
82
) to bound

\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{t}-\xi_{2}^{t}\|^{4}
shows (
84
).
∎

Lemma 3.8
.

Fix any
T,C_{0}>0
. For
i=1,2
, suppose

Y_{i}=(C_{\theta,i},R_{\theta,i})\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
, and
let
(C_{f,i},R_{f,i},R_{f,i}^{*},\Gamma_{i})=\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}(Y_{i})
. Then there exist
constants
C\equiv C(T,C_{0})>0
and
\lambda_{0}\equiv\lambda_{0}(T,C_{0})>0

such that for any
\lambda>\lambda_{0}
,

\begin{gathered}\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y_{1},Y_{2}),\\
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1},R_{f,2}),\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1}^{*},R_{f,2}^{*}),\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})\leq C\,\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y_{1},Y_{2}).\end{gathered}

Proof.

Let
\{w^{t}_{1},w^{t}_{2}\}_{t\in[0,T]\cup\{*\}}
be a coupling
of
\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{\theta,1})
and
\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{\theta,2})
that satisfies

(85)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}(\mathbb{E}\|w^{t}_{1}-w^{t}_{2}\|^{4})^{1/4}\leq 2\,\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2}).

Then by (
82
) and the definition of
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\cdot,\cdot)
,

e^{-4\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{t}-\xi_{2}^{t}\|^{4}\lesssim\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{\theta,1},R_{\theta,2})^{4}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})^{4}\lesssim\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y_{1},Y_{2})^{4}.

For
i=1,2
, define

h_{i}^{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\xi_{i}^{r-},w^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}.

Then by the Lipschitz property of
f
and (
74
),

\mathbb{E}\|h_{1}^{t}-h_{2}^{t}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{r}-\xi_{2}^{r}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\leq\sup_{r\in[0,t]}e^{-2\lambda r}\,\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{r}-\xi_{2}^{r}\|^{2}\cdot\int_{0}^{t}e^{2\lambda r}\mathrm{d}r\lesssim\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y_{1},Y_{2})^{2}\cdot\int_{0}^{t}e^{2\lambda r}\mathrm{d}r,

so

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\|h_{1}^{t}-h_{2}^{t}\|^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda}\,\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y_{1},Y_{2})^{2}.

Letting
\{u_{1}^{t},u_{2}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be a jointly Gaussian process with the
same covariance as
\{h_{1}^{t},h_{2}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, this gives a coupling of

\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{f,1})
and
\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{f,2})
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}\mathbb{E}{\|u^{t}_{1}-u^{t}_{2}\|^{4}}\lesssim\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}(\mathbb{E}{\|u^{t}_{1}-u^{t}_{2}\|^{2}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}(\mathbb{E}{\|h^{t}_{1}-h^{t}_{2}\|^{2}})^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\,\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y_{1},Y_{2})^{4}.

For
R_{f}
, note that by (
83
),

\displaystyle\|R_{f,1}^{t}-R_{f,2}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}=\sup_{x:\|x\|_{L^{4}}\leq 1}\|\mathbb{E}r_{f,1}^{t}(x^{[t]})-\mathbb{E}r_{f,2}^{t}(x^{[t]})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{2}+(\mathbb{E}\|w_{1}^{t}-w_{2}^{t}\|^{4})^{1/2}+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|w_{1}^{r}-w_{2}^{r}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\|R_{\theta,1}^{t,r}-R_{\theta,2}^{t,r}\|^{2}+\sup_{r\in[0,t]}(e^{-4\lambda r}\mathbb{E}\|w_{1}^{r}-w_{2}^{r}\|^{4})^{1/2}\left(e^{2\lambda t}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}e^{4\lambda r}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{1/2}\right).

Multiplying by
e^{-2\lambda t}
and applying (
85
) shows

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1},R_{f,2})^{2}\lesssim\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{\theta,1},R_{\theta,2})^{2}+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})^{2}\lesssim\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y_{1},Y_{2})^{2}.

A similar argument using (
84
)
and
\|\Gamma_{1}^{t}-\Gamma_{2}^{t}\|\lesssim\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}^{t}-\xi_{2}^{t}\|
shows

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{f,1}^{*},R_{f,2}^{*})\lesssim\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y_{1},Y_{2})

and
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})\lesssim\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y_{1},Y_{2})
.
∎

We now conclude the proof of
2.5
.

Proof of
2.5
(b).

Fix
T>0
, and any constant
C_{0}>0
large enough.
By Lemmas
3.3
and
3.4
,

\mathcal{T}:=\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}\circ\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}

defines a mapping from
\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}\equiv\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}(T,C_{0})
to itself. For
i=1,2
, consider any

Y_{i}=(C_{\theta,i},R_{\theta,i})\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}
,
let
X_{i}=(C_{f,i},R_{f,i},R_{f,i}^{*},\Gamma_{i})=\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}(Y_{i})
.
Then by Lemmas
3.6
and
3.8
,
for any large enough
\lambda>0
,

(86)

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\mathcal{T}(Y_{1}),\mathcal{T}(Y_{2}))\lesssim\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f,1},C_{f,2})+\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(X_{1},X_{2})\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y_{1},Y_{2}).

Thus for large enough
\lambda>0
, we have

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\mathcal{T}(Y_{1}),\mathcal{T}(Y_{2}))\leq\frac{1}{2}\,\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y_{1},Y_{2})
,
implying that
\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}

is contractive in the metric
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\cdot,\cdot)
.

It is clear that
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{\theta,1},R_{\theta,2})
defines a complete metric
on the space of Borel-measurable
\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
-valued
processes
\{R_{\theta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}
, and the conditions in
Definition
3.1
for

\{R_{\theta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}
to belong to
\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}
are
closed under
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{\theta,1},R_{\theta,2})
. For the metric

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})
, define also

\|C_{\theta,1}-C_{\theta,2}\|_{\infty}=\sup_{t,s\in[0,T]\cup\{*\}}|C_{\theta,1}^{t,s}-C_{\theta,2}^{t,s}|.

We have by Cauchy-Schwarz that
for any
C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2}
satisfying the conditions of

\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}
,

\displaystyle\|C_{\theta,1}-C_{\theta,2}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{w_{1},w_{2}\sim\gamma\in\Gamma(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{\|w_{1}^{t}\|^{2}}\right]\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\|w_{1}^{s}-w_{2}^{s}\|^{2}}\right]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{\|w_{2}^{s}\|^{2}}\right]\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\|w_{1}^{t}-w_{2}^{t}\|^{2}}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(T)}e^{\lambda T}\cdot\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{\theta,1},R_{\theta,2}).

Conversely, by the condition (
62
) and

[
31
, Lemma D.1]
, there exists a jointly
Gaussian coupling
(w_{1},w_{2})\sim\gamma\in\Gamma(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})

for which

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}(\mathbb{E}\|w_{1}^{t}-w_{2}^{t}\|^{4})^{1/4}\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}(\mathbb{E}\|w_{1}^{t}-w_{2}^{t}\|^{2})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\Big(\sqrt{\|C_{\theta,1}-C_{\theta,2}\|_{\infty}}+\|C_{\theta,1}-C_{\theta,2}\|_{\infty}\Big)^{1/2},

where
C,C^{\prime}>0
are some constants depending on
T,C_{0},k
. Thus, the metrics

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})
and

\|C_{\theta,1}-C_{\theta,2}\|_{\infty}
are uniformly equivalent over the space
of covariance kernels
\{C_{\theta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]\cup\{*\}}

satisfying the conditions of
\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}
. Since this space is
complete under
\|C_{\theta,1}-C_{\theta,2}\|_{\infty}
, it is also complete under

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta,1},C_{\theta,2})
.

Then
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\cdot,\cdot)
defines a complete metric on

\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}
, so by the Banach fixed-point theorem, there exists a
unique fixed point
Y\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{cont}}_{\theta}
such that

\mathcal{T}(Y)=Y
. Since the image of
\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{S}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\theta}

is contained in
\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}
, this fixed point is also unique in

\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
. Finally, this implies that for any

C_{0}>0
large enough, there is a unique fixed point

(C_{\theta},R_{\theta},C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)\in\mathcal{S}\equiv\mathcal{S}(T,C_{0})
satisfying
(
18
–
23
).
∎

4.
DMFT approximation in discrete time

In this section, we introduce and analyze
a time-discretized version of the SGD and SME
processes: Fixing a discretization size
\delta>0
, let
\{\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be i.i.d. random vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
encapsulating the data sampling
noise, with law

\begin{cases}\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{t}=\bm{\Pi}\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{t}&\text{for SGD},\\
\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(\delta\bar{\kappa}1_{n},\delta\bar{\kappa}\,\mathrm{Id}_{n\times n})&\text{for SME}.\end{cases}

In the SGD setting,
\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{{n\choose\kappa}}
is a random vector
with i.i.d.
\operatorname{Poisson}(\delta n^{1-\alpha}/{n\choose\kappa})
entries,
and
\bm{\Pi}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times{n\choose\kappa}}
is an incidence matrix having
entries

\Pi_{i,S}=\mathbf{1}_{i\in S}\text{ for all $i\in[n]$ and $S\subset\mathcal{S}$}.

Note that marginally, each entry of
\bm{\Pi}\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{t}
has law

\operatorname{Poisson}(\binom{n-1}{\kappa-1}\cdot\delta n^{1-\alpha}/{n\choose\kappa})=\operatorname{Poisson}(\delta\kappa/n^{\alpha})
,
where
\delta\kappa/n^{\alpha}\approx\delta\bar{\kappa}

for large
n
under Assumption
2.1
.

For both SGD and SME, we consider the discrete-time dynamics

\displaystyle{\bm{\theta}}_{\delta}^{t+1}

\displaystyle={\bm{\theta}}_{\delta}^{t}-\frac{\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{i}\otimes\Big(f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{t},\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*},\varepsilon_{i})\cdot z_{\delta,i}^{t}\Big)-\delta\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}g(\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{t})

(87)

\displaystyle={\bm{\theta}}_{\delta}^{t}-\frac{\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\big(f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{t},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\odot\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{t}\big)-\delta\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}g({\bm{\theta}}_{\delta}^{t}).

Here and throughout,
\mathbf{X}=[\mathbf{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{n}]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
,

f:\mathbb{R}^{n\times(k+k^{*}+1)}\to\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}

and
g:\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}
denote the applications of

f(\cdot)
and
g(\cdot)
row-wise, and
\odot
denotes the row-wise
scalar product. We will write also

{\bm{\xi}}_{\delta}^{t}=\mathbf{X}{\bm{\theta}}_{\delta}^{t},\qquad\bm{\xi}^{*}=\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}.

We remark that these discrete-time dynamics (
87
)
may be understood as a variant of SGD/SME where the stochastic
gradient or drift/diffusion coefficients are only updated in (rescaled) time
increments of
\delta
, i.e. they are computed in (
7
)
and (
8
) using
\bm{\theta}^{k\delta}
instead of
\bm{\theta}^{t}
if
t\in[k\delta,(k+1)\delta)
.

We show in Section
4.1
that
these discrete-time dynamics are characterized by a system of discrete-time
DMFT equations as
n,d\to\infty
, and in Section

4.2
that this discrete-time DMFT system converges
to the continuous-time system defined in Section
2.3
as

\delta\to 0
. This proof strategy was introduced in
[
18
]
, and our
arguments will follow closely its implementation in
[
30
]
.

4.1.
Discrete DMFT approximation via AMP

Given the same variables
\theta^{0},\theta^{*},\varepsilon
as in
(
10
), the discrete DMFT system is defined by the primary
processes

(88)

\displaystyle\theta^{t}_{\delta}

\displaystyle=\theta^{0}-\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}\left({{\gamma}\Gamma_{\delta}^{r}\theta^{r}_{\delta}+g(\theta^{r}_{\delta})+\gamma\sum_{q=0}^{r-1}R_{f,\delta}^{r,q}\theta^{q}_{\delta}+{\gamma}R_{f,\delta}^{r,*}\theta^{*}}\right)+\sqrt{\gamma}u_{\delta}^{t},

(89)

\displaystyle\xi^{t}_{\delta}

\displaystyle={-}\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}f(\xi_{\delta}^{r},w^{*},\varepsilon)z^{r}_{\delta}+w^{t}_{\delta},

and the response processes

\displaystyle r_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Id}_{k}-\sum_{r=s}^{t-1}\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}\bigg[\left({\gamma}\Gamma_{\delta}^{r}+\mathrm{D}g(\theta_{\delta}^{r})\right)\cdot r_{\theta,\delta}^{r,s}+{\gamma}\sum_{q=s+1}^{r-1}R_{f,\delta}^{r,q}r_{\theta,\delta}^{q,s}\bigg]\text{ for }s<t,

(90)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999ptr_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}=0\text{ for }s\geq t,

(91)

\displaystyle r_{f,\delta}^{t,*}

\displaystyle={-}\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{t}_{\delta},w^{*},\varepsilon)\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}r_{f,\delta}^{r,*}z^{r}_{\delta}+\mathrm{D}_{w^{*}}f(\xi^{t}_{\delta},w^{*},\varepsilon),

\displaystyle r_{f,\delta}^{t,s}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)\left({\sum_{r=s+1}^{t-1}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}r_{f,\delta}^{r,s}z^{r}_{\delta}+\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\cdot\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{\delta}^{s},w^{*},\varepsilon)z^{s}_{\delta}}\right)\text{ for }s<t,

(92)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999ptr_{f,\delta}^{t,s}=0\text{ for }s\geq t,

where

\{u_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\sim\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{\theta,\delta}),\qquad(\{w_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0},w^{*})\sim\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{f,\delta})

are discrete-time Gaussian processes,

\{z_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\overset{iid}{\sim}\begin{cases}\operatorname{Poisson}(\delta\bar{\kappa})&\text{ for SGD},\\
\mathcal{N}(\delta\bar{\kappa},\delta\bar{\kappa})&\text{ for SME},\end{cases}

and these are independent of each other and of
\theta^{0},\theta^{*},\varepsilon
.
The deterministic correlation and response kernels are defined as
expectations with respect to the above laws,

(93)

\displaystyle C_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\theta^{t}_{\delta}\otimes\theta^{s}_{\delta}]\text{ for }t,s\in[0,\infty)\cup\{*\}

(94)

\displaystyle C_{f,\delta}^{t,s}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\sum_{r=0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\xi_{\delta}^{r},w^{*},\varepsilon)z^{r}_{\delta}\otimes\sum_{r=0}^{s}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\xi_{\delta}^{s},w^{*},\varepsilon)z^{s}_{\delta}}\right]

(95)

\displaystyle R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[r_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}]

(96)

\displaystyle R_{f,\delta}^{t,s}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[r_{f,\delta}^{t,s}]

(97)

\displaystyle R_{f,\delta}^{t,*}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[r_{f,\delta}^{t,*}]

(98)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{t}_{\delta}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi^{t}_{\delta},w^{*},\varepsilon)]

We clarify that in contrast to the continuous-time setting, it is immediate to
see that these processes/kernels are uniquely defined recursively in time via

\begin{gathered}\{\theta_{\delta}^{t},r_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\}_{s,t=0}\mapsto\{C_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s},R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\}_{s,t=0}\mapsto\{\xi_{\delta}^{t},r_{f,\delta}^{t,s},r_{f,\delta}^{t,*}\}_{s,t=0}\mapsto\{C_{f,\delta}^{t,s},R_{f,\delta}^{t,s},R_{f,\delta}^{t,*},\Gamma_{\delta}^{t}\}_{s,t=0}\mapsto\\
\{\theta_{\delta}^{t},r_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\}_{s,t\leq 1}\mapsto\{C_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s},R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\}_{s,t\leq 1}\mapsto\{\xi_{\delta}^{t},r_{f,\delta}^{t,s},r_{f,\delta}^{t,*}\}_{s,t\leq 1}\mapsto\{C_{f,\delta}^{t,s},R_{f,\delta}^{t,s},R_{f,\delta}^{t,*},\Gamma_{\delta}^{t}\}_{s,t\leq 1}\mapsto\ldots\end{gathered}

The main result of this section is the following lemma, which shows that
this discrete-time DMFT system approximates the dynamics
(
87
) in the large
n,d
limit.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\{\theta_{\delta,j}^{t},\theta_{j}^{*}\}
be the rows of

\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{t},\bm{\theta}^{*}
, and let
\{\xi_{\delta,i}^{t},\xi_{i}^{*},\varepsilon_{i}\}
be
those of
\bm{\xi}_{\delta}^{t},\bm{\xi}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}
.
Then for any fixed integer
T\geq 0
, almost surely as
n,d\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta^{0}_{\delta,j},\ldots,\theta^{T}_{\delta,j},\theta^{\ast}_{j})}\Rightarrow\operatorname{Law}\big(\theta^{0}_{\delta},\ldots,\theta^{T}_{\delta},\theta^{\ast}\big),\quad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{(\xi^{0}_{\delta,i},\ldots,\xi^{T}_{\delta,i},\xi_{i}^{*},\varepsilon_{i})}\Rightarrow\operatorname{Law}\big(\xi^{0}_{\delta},\ldots,\xi^{T}_{\delta},w^{*},\varepsilon\big)

weakly and in Wasserstein-2, where the right side denotes the joint law of these
variables defined via the discrete-time DMFT recursions
(
88
–
98
).

We proceed to prove Lemma
4.1
,
by re-expressing the dynamics (
87
) as an Approximate
Message Passing (AMP) procedure
for which rigorous state evolution results are available.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
\{\theta_{j}^{0},\theta_{j}^{*}\}
and

\{\varepsilon_{i},z_{\delta,i}^{t}\}
denote the rows of

\bm{\theta}^{0},\bm{\theta}^{*}
and
\bm{\varepsilon},\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{t}
.
Then for any fixed integer
T\geq 0
, almost surely as
n,d\to\infty
,

\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{0},\theta_{j}^{*})}\Rightarrow\operatorname{Law}(\theta^{0},\theta^{*})\qquad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{(\varepsilon_{i},z_{\delta,i}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta,i}^{T})}\Rightarrow\operatorname{Law}(\varepsilon,z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{T})

weakly and in Wasserstein-
p
for each fixed order
p\geq 1
.

Proof.

The convergence for
(\bm{\theta}^{0},\bm{\theta}^{*})
is assumed in
Assumption
2.2
(c).
In the SME setting, the convergence for

(\bm{\varepsilon},\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{T})
is also immediate from Assumption

2.2
(c) and the independence of entries of
\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{t}
,
see e.g.
[
32
, Proposition E.1]
.

In the setting of SGD, fix any
k\geq 0
and
monic monomial
q:\mathbb{R}^{T+1}\to\mathbb{R}
, and consider

Q=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{k}q(z_{\delta,i}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta,i}^{T})

as a polynomial function of the i.i.d. Poisson variables

\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{P}_{0}^{T}
defining

\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{t}=\bm{\Pi}\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{t}
. Note that since
\bm{\Pi}
has nonnegative entries,
each term
q(z_{\delta,i}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta,i}^{T})
is then some polynomial of
the coordinates of
\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{T}
with
nonnegative coefficients. By the concentration inequality of

[
82
, Theorem 1.4, Lemma 7.6]
, for any
\lambda\geq 0
, we have

(99)

\mathbb{P}[|Q-\mathbb{E}Q|\geq\lambda]\leq C\max\left\{\max_{r=1,\ldots,\deg(q)}e^{-\frac{c\lambda^{2}}{\mu_{0}\mu_{r}}},\max_{r=1,\ldots,\deg(q)}e^{-\left(\frac{c\lambda}{\mu_{r}}\right)^{1/r}}\right\}

for some
(k,q)
-dependent constants
C,c>0
. Here
\deg(q)
is the degree of

q
, and (c.f.
[
82
, Section 1.4]
)

\mu_{0}\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\varepsilon_{i}|^{k}\mathbb{E}q(z_{\delta,i}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta,i}^{T}),\qquad\mu_{r}\leq\max_{|S|=r}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{S}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\varepsilon_{i}|^{k}q(z_{\delta,i}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta,i}^{T})\right]

where this bound for
\mu_{r}
denotes the maximum expected mixed partial
derivative of order
r
in any combination of the coordinates of

\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{T}
. Since
each coordinate of
\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{T}
can influence at most

\kappa
samples
i=1,\ldots,n
, it is clear by Assumption
2.2
(c) that
for all large
n,d
, each
r\geq 1
, and some
(k,q,r)
-dependent constants

C,C^{\prime}>0
, we have

\mu_{0}\leq C,\quad\mu_{r}\leq\max_{S\subset[n]:|S|=\kappa}\frac{C}{n}\sum_{i\in S}|\varepsilon_{i}|^{k}\leq\max_{S\subset[n]:|S|=\kappa}C\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\varepsilon_{i}|^{2k}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{i\in S}\right)^{1/2}\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa}{n}}.

Since
\kappa\asymp n^{\alpha}

and
\alpha<1
, applying these bounds to (
99
) shows,
by the Borel-Cantelli lemma,
that there exists a constant
\iota>0
for which
|Q-\mathbb{E}Q|<n^{-\iota}

a.s. for all large
n,d
. On the other hand, letting

\{\tilde{z}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\overset{iid}{\sim}\operatorname{Poisson}(\delta\kappa/n^{\alpha})
and

\{z_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\overset{iid}{\sim}\operatorname{Poisson}(\delta\bar{\kappa})
, by Assumptions
2.1
and
2.2

we have

\mathbb{E}Q=\mathbb{E}q(\tilde{z}_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,\tilde{z}_{\delta}^{T})\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{k}=\mathbb{E}q(z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{T})\cdot\mathbb{E}\varepsilon^{k}+o(1).

This shows that
Q
converges a.s. to
\mathbb{E}q(z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{T})\cdot\mathbb{E}\varepsilon^{k}
, i.e. each mixed moment of the empirical measure

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{(\varepsilon_{i},z_{\delta,i}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta,i}^{T})}

converges a.s. to that of
\operatorname{Law}(\varepsilon,z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{T})
.
Since
\varepsilon
has finite moment generating function around 0, so
does
