(\varepsilon,z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{T})
. Then this convergence of mixed
moments implies convergence weakly and in Wasserstein-
p

[
87
, Thm 6.9]
, establishing the lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

The proof relies on mapping the dynamics (
87
) to an
AMP algorithm: Let us set

\mathbf{V}^{0}=[\mathbf{V}_{1}^{0},\mathbf{V}_{2}^{0}]:=[\bm{\theta}^{0},\bm{\theta}^{*}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times(k+k^{*})},\quad\mathbf{V}^{t}=[\mathbf{V}_{1}^{t},\mathbf{V}_{2}^{t}]:=[\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{t}-\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{t-1},0]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times(k+k^{*})}\text{ for }t\geq 1,

\mathbf{Y}^{t}=[\mathbf{Y}_{1}^{t},\mathbf{Y}_{2}^{t}]:=\left[{-}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{t},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\odot\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{t},\,0\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times(k+k^{*})}\text{ for }t\geq 0.

Then

(100)

[\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{t},\bm{\theta}^{*}]=\sum_{r=0}^{t}\mathbf{V}^{r}.

For any matrices
A^{t,q},B^{t,q}\in\mathbb{R}^{(k+k^{*})\times(k+k^{*})}
, the dynamics (
87
) are then equivalent to the
iterations, for
t=0,1,2,\ldots

(101)

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V}^{t}-\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\mathbf{Y}^{r}(A^{t,r})^{\top},

(102)

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}^{t}

\displaystyle=\left[{-}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}f\left(\sum_{q=0}^{t}\left(\mathbf{W}^{q}+\sum_{r=0}^{q-1}\mathbf{Y}^{r}(A^{q,r})^{\top}\right),\bm{\varepsilon}\right)\odot\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{t},\;0\right],

(103)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}^{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}^{t}-\sum_{q=0}^{t}\mathbf{V}^{q}(B^{t,q})^{\top},

(104)

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{U}^{t}+\sum_{q=0}^{t}\mathbf{V}^{q}(B^{t,q})^{\top}-\left[\delta\bar{\eta}^{\delta t}g\left(\sum_{q=0}^{t}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{q}\right),\,0\right].

We may identify (
102
) and (
104
) as two mappings

(105)

\mathbf{Y}^{t}=y_{t}(\mathbf{W}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{W}^{t},\bm{\varepsilon},\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{t}),\qquad\mathbf{V}^{t+1}=v_{t+1}(\mathbf{U}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{U}^{t},\mathbf{V}^{0})

defined recursively for
t=0,1,2,\ldots
via

(106)

\displaystyle y_{t}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{t},\varepsilon,z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{t})

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}{-}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}z_{\delta}^{t}f\left(\sum_{q=0}^{t}\left(W^{q}+\sum_{r=0}^{q-1}A^{q,r}y_{r}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{r},\varepsilon,z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{r})\right),\varepsilon\right)\\
0\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle v_{t+1}(U^{0},\ldots,U^{t},V^{0})

\displaystyle=U^{t}+B^{t,0}V^{0}+\sum_{q=1}^{t}B^{t,q}v_{q}(U^{0},\ldots,U^{q-1},V^{0})

(107)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt-\begin{pmatrix}\delta\bar{\eta}^{\delta t}g\left(V_{1}^{0}+\sum_{q=1}^{t}[v_{q}(U^{0},\ldots,U^{q-1},V^{0})]_{1}\right)\\
0\end{pmatrix},

and applied row-wise to their inputs in (
105
).
Let
\theta^{0},\theta^{*},\varepsilon,\{z_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be as defined in the
discrete-time DMFT system. We set

V^{0}=(\theta^{0},\theta^{*})\in\mathbb{R}^{k+k^{*}}

and define recursively for
t=0,1,2,\ldots

(108)

\begin{gathered}A^{t,q}=\mathbb{E}\mathrm{D}_{q}V^{t}\text{ for all }q\in\{0,\ldots,t-1\},\quad\Sigma^{t}=\mathbb{E}[(V^{0},\ldots,V^{t})\otimes(V^{0},\ldots,V^{t})],\\
(W^{0},\ldots,W^{t})\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma^{t})\text{ (independent of
$\varepsilon,\{z_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}$)},\quad Y^{t}=y_{t}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{t},\varepsilon,z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{t}),\\
B^{t,q}=\gamma\,\mathbb{E}\mathrm{D}_{q}Y^{t}\text{ for all }s\in\{0,\ldots,t\},\quad\Omega^{t}=\gamma\,\mathbb{E}[(Y^{0},\ldots,Y^{t})\otimes(Y^{0}\ldots,Y^{t})],\\
(U^{0},\ldots,U^{t})\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\Omega^{t})\text{ (independent of $\theta^{0},\theta^{*}$)},\quad V^{t+1}=v_{t+1}(U^{0},\ldots,U^{t},V^{0}),\end{gathered}

where
\mathrm{D}_{q}V^{t}\equiv\mathrm{D}_{U^{q}}v_{t}(U^{0},\ldots,U^{t-1},V^{0})\in\mathbb{R}^{(k+k^{*})\times(k+k^{*})}
denotes the partial derivative (Jacobian) in
U^{q}
, and
similarly
\mathrm{D}_{q}Y^{t}\equiv\mathrm{D}_{W^{q}}y_{t}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{t},\varepsilon,z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{t})\in\mathbb{R}^{(k+k^{*})\times(k+k^{*})}
denotes that in
W^{q}
. Then, using these choices of

\{A^{t,q},B^{t,s}\}
, (
101
–
104
) is a standard form of an
AMP algorithm with state evolution covariances
\{\Sigma^{t},\Omega^{t}\}
, see
e.g.
[
45
]
.

We apply the result of
[
89
, Theorem 2.21]
for this AMP
algorithm: It is clear that

T
iterations of (
101
–
104
) may be directly mapped to

(k+k^{*})T
vector iterations of

[
89
, Eqs. (2.14) and (D.1–D.2)]
with
side information vectors
(\bm{\varepsilon},\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{T})

and the notational identification
(1/\sqrt{\gamma})\mathbf{X}\leftrightarrow\mathbf{W}^{\top}
.
Each function

y_{t}(\cdot)
and
v_{t+1}(\cdot)
recursively defined via (
102
) and
(
104
) has polynomial growth and is Lipschitz in its first
t+1

arguments
W^{0},\ldots,W^{t}
and
U^{0},\ldots,U^{t}
, by the conditions for

f(\cdot)
and
g(\cdot)
in Assumption
2.3
.
3
3

3

Here,

(W^{0},\ldots,W^{t})\mapsto y(W^{0},\ldots,W^{t},\varepsilon,z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{t})

is
(\prod_{s=0}^{t}C\max(1,|z_{\delta}^{s}|))
-Lipschitz where
C>0
depends on the
Lipschitz constant of
f
. The argument of
[
89
, Theorem
2.21]
implicitly assumes that

(W^{0},\ldots,W^{t})\mapsto y(W^{0},\ldots,W^{t},\varepsilon,z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{t})

is
L
-Lipschitz for a deterministic constant
L>0
, but it is clear from the
proof that the argument holds as long as this is

L(\varepsilon,z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{t})
-Lipschitz where

\mathbb{E}|L(\varepsilon,z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{t})|^{2+\iota}<\infty
for some
\iota>0
.

The conditions of
[
89
, Definition 2.18]
hold for
the data matrix

(1/\sqrt{\gamma})\mathbf{X}
by Assumption
2.2
, with constant
variance profile across all entries, and
[
89
, Assumption
2.17]
is verified by Lemma
4.2
.
Then by
[
89
, Theorem 2.21]
, for any fixed
T\geq 1
,
almost surely as
n,d\to\infty
,

(109)

\begin{gathered}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(U_{j}^{0},\ldots,U_{j}^{T},V_{j}^{0},\ldots,V_{j}^{T})}\Rightarrow\operatorname{Law}(U^{0},\ldots,U^{T},V^{0},\ldots,V^{T}),\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{(W_{i}^{0},\ldots,W_{i}^{T},Y_{i}^{0},\ldots,Y_{i}^{T},\varepsilon_{i},z_{\delta,i}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta,i}^{T})}\Rightarrow\operatorname{Law}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{T},Y^{0},\ldots,Y^{T},\varepsilon,z_{\delta}^{0},\ldots,z_{\delta}^{T})\end{gathered}

weakly and in Wasserstein-2.

Finally, we show that this implies the statements of the lemma.
Writing the two components of
V^{t},Y^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k^{*}}
as

V^{t}=(V_{1}^{t},V_{2}^{t})
and
Y^{t}=(Y_{1}^{t},Y_{2}^{t})
, note that by the definitions
(

) and (

) we have

V^{t}=(V_{1}^{t},0)\text{ for all }t\geq 1,\quad Y^{t}=(Y_{1}^{t},0)\text{ for all }t\geq 0,

and hence also

A^{t,q}=\begin{pmatrix}(A^{t,q})_{11}&(A^{t,q})_{12}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix},\;W_{2}^{t}=0\text{ for all }t\geq 1,

B^{t,q}=\begin{pmatrix}(B^{t,q})_{11}&(B^{t,q})_{12}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix},\;U_{2}^{t}=0\text{ for all }t\geq 0.

Let us define

(110)

\displaystyle(\theta_{\delta}^{t},\theta^{*})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{r=0}^{t}V^{r}=\left(\sum_{r=0}^{t}V_{1}^{r},\,V_{2}^{0}\right),

(111)

\displaystyle(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{q=0}^{t}\left(W^{q}+\sum_{r=0}^{q-1}A^{q,r}Y^{r}\right)=\left(\sum_{q=0}^{t}W_{1}^{q}+\sum_{q=1}^{t}\sum_{r=0}^{q-1}(A^{q,r})_{11}Y_{1}^{r},\,W_{2}^{0}\right).

Comparing these definitions with
(
100
) and (
101
), the convergence
(
109
) implies that also

(112)

\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta^{0}_{\delta,j},\ldots,\theta^{T}_{\delta,j},\theta^{\ast}_{j})}\Rightarrow\operatorname{Law}\big(\theta^{0}_{\delta},\ldots,\theta^{T}_{\delta},\theta^{\ast}\big),\quad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{(\xi^{0}_{\delta,i},\ldots,\xi^{T}_{\delta,i},\xi_{i}^{*},\varepsilon_{i})}\Rightarrow\operatorname{Law}\big(\xi^{0}_{\delta},\ldots,\xi^{T}_{\delta},w^{*},\varepsilon\big).

So it remains to check that these definitions of

\theta_{\delta}^{t},\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*}
in (

–

)
coincide with those defined by the discrete DMFT recursions
(

–

).

Differentiating (
106
)
and (
107
) by the chain rule and applying
A^{q,r}=\mathbb{E}\mathrm{D}_{r}V^{q}

and
B^{t,s}=\gamma\,\mathbb{E}\mathrm{D}_{s}Y^{t}
, observe that

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{s}Y^{t}

\displaystyle:=\begin{pmatrix}(\mathrm{D}_{s}Y^{t})_{11}&(\mathrm{D}_{s}Y^{t})_{12}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}

(113)

\displaystyle={-}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}z_{\delta}^{t}\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)&\mathrm{D}_{w^{*}}f(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\left(\mathrm{Id}_{k+k^{*}}+\sum_{q=s+1}^{t}\sum_{r=s}^{q-1}(\mathbb{E}\mathrm{D}_{r}V^{q})(\mathrm{D}_{s}Y^{r})\right),

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{s}V^{t+1}

\displaystyle:=\begin{pmatrix}(\mathrm{D}_{s}V^{t+1})_{11}&(\mathrm{D}_{s}V^{t+1})_{12}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Id}_{k+k^{*}}\mathbf{1}_{s=t}+\sum_{q=s+1}^{t}(\gamma\mathbb{E}\mathrm{D}_{q}Y^{t})(\mathrm{D}_{s}V^{q})

(114)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt-\delta\bar{\eta}^{\delta t}\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{D}g(\theta_{\delta}^{t})\sum_{q=s+1}^{t}(\mathrm{D}_{s}V^{q})_{11}&\mathrm{D}g(\theta_{\delta}^{t})\sum_{q=s+1}^{t}(\mathrm{D}_{s}V^{q})_{12}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}.

In particular, for
s=t
we have
(\mathrm{D}_{t}Y^{t})_{11}={-}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}z_{\delta}^{t}\cdot\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)
. Let us define

(115)

\begin{gathered}(\mathrm{D}_{s}Y^{t}-\mathrm{D}_{s+1}Y^{t})_{11}:={-}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}z_{\delta}^{t}r_{f,\delta}^{t,s},\quad(\mathrm{D}_{0}Y^{t})_{12}:={-}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}z_{\delta}^{t}r_{f,\delta}^{t,*},\quad R_{f,\delta}^{t,s}=\mathbb{E}r_{f,\delta}^{t,s},\quad R_{f,\delta}^{t,*}=\mathbb{E}r_{f,\delta}^{t,*},\\
\sum_{r=s+1}^{t}(\mathrm{D}_{s}V^{r})_{11}=\sum_{r=s}^{t-1}(\mathrm{D}_{s}V^{r+1})_{11}:=r_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s},\quad R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}=\mathbb{E}r_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s},\quad\Gamma_{\delta}^{t}=\mathbb{E}\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon),\end{gathered}

and apply the identities

\displaystyle\sum_{q=s+1}^{t}\sum_{r=s}^{q-1}[(\mathbb{E}\mathrm{D}_{r}V^{q})(\mathrm{D}_{s}Y^{r})]_{1i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=s}^{t-1}\left(\sum_{q=r+1}^{t}\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{D}_{r}V^{q})_{11}\right)(\mathrm{D}_{s}Y^{r})_{1i}

(116)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=s}^{t-1}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}(\mathrm{D}_{s}Y^{r})_{1i}\text{ for }i=1,2,

\displaystyle\sum_{q=s+1}^{t}[(\mathbb{E}\mathrm{D}_{q}Y^{t})(\mathrm{D}_{s}V^{q})]_{11}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=s+1}^{t}\left(\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{D}_{t}Y^{t})_{11}+\sum_{r=q}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{D}_{r}Y^{t}-\mathrm{D}_{r+1}Y^{t})_{11}\right)(\mathrm{D}_{s}V^{q})_{11}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{D}_{t}Y^{t})_{11}\sum_{q=s+1}^{t}(\mathrm{D}_{s}V^{q})_{11}+\sum_{r=s+1}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{D}_{r}Y^{t}-\mathrm{D}_{r+1}Y^{t})_{11}\left(\sum_{q=s+1}^{r}(\mathrm{D}_{s}V^{q})_{11}\right)

(117)

\displaystyle={-}\delta\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}\Gamma_{\delta}^{t}r_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}-\delta\bar{\eta}^{t\delta}\sum_{r=s+1}^{t-1}R_{f,\delta}^{t,r}r_{\theta,\delta}^{r,s}.

In the last step, we have used
\mathbb{E}z_{\delta}^{t}=\delta\bar{\kappa}
and the fact
that
z_{\delta}^{t}
is independent of

\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)
and
r_{f,\delta}^{t,s}
.
Then, applying (
115
),
(
116
), (
117
) to
(

–
114
), we obtain the recursions

\displaystyle r_{f,\delta}^{t,s}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)\left(\sum_{r=s+1}^{t-1}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}(\mathrm{D}_{s}Y^{r}-\mathrm{D}_{s+1}Y^{r})_{11}+R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}(\mathrm{D}_{s}Y^{s})_{11}\right)

\displaystyle={-}\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)\left(\sum_{r=s+1}^{t-1}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}r_{f,\delta}^{r,s}z_{\delta}^{r}+\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\cdot\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{\delta}^{s},w^{*},\varepsilon)z_{\delta}^{s}\right),

\displaystyle r_{f,\delta}^{t,*}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{D}_{w^{*}}f(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)+\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}(\mathrm{D}_{0}Y^{r})_{12}

\displaystyle={-}\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon)\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}r_{f,\delta}^{r,*}z_{\delta}^{r}+\mathrm{D}_{w^{*}}f(\xi_{\delta}^{t},w^{*},\varepsilon),

\displaystyle r_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=s}^{t-1}\left(\mathrm{Id}_{k}\mathbf{1}_{s=r}-\gamma\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}\Gamma_{\delta}^{r}r_{\theta,\delta}^{r,s}-\gamma\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}\sum_{q=s+1}^{r-1}R_{f,\delta}^{r,q}r_{\theta,\delta}^{q,s}\right)-\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}\mathrm{D}g(\theta_{\delta}^{r})r_{\theta,\delta}^{r,s}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Id}_{k}-\sum_{r=s}^{t-1}\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}\bigg[(\gamma\Gamma_{\delta}^{r}+\mathrm{D}g(\theta_{\delta}^{r}))\cdot r_{\theta,\delta}^{r,s}+\gamma\sum_{q=s+1}^{r-1}R_{f,\delta}^{r,q}r_{\theta,\delta}^{q,s}\bigg]

which are precisely (
90
–
92
).
Furthermore, let us define

(118)

\sum_{r=0}^{t}U_{1}^{r}:=\sqrt{\gamma}u_{\delta}^{t},\qquad\sum_{q=0}^{t}W_{1}^{q}:=w_{\delta}^{t}.

Then by (
110
) and (
107
),

\displaystyle\theta_{\delta}^{t}=V_{1}^{0}+\sum_{r=1}^{t}V_{1}^{r}

\displaystyle=\theta^{0}+\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\left(U_{1}^{r}+\sum_{q=0}^{r}(B^{r,q})_{11}V_{1}^{r}+(B^{r,0})_{12}V_{2}^{0}-\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}g(\theta_{\delta}^{r})\right)

\displaystyle=\theta^{0}+\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\left(U_{1}^{r}+\gamma\sum_{q=0}^{r}(\mathbb{E}\mathrm{D}_{q}Y^{r})_{11}V_{1}^{r}+\gamma(\mathbb{E}\mathrm{D}_{0}Y^{r})_{12}V_{2}^{0}-\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}g(\theta_{\delta}^{r})\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{=}\theta^{0}+\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\left(U_{1}^{r}+\gamma\left({-}\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}\Gamma_{\delta}^{r}\theta_{\delta}^{r}-\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}\sum_{q=0}^{r-1}R_{f,\delta}^{r,q}\theta_{\delta}^{q}-\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}R_{f,\delta}^{t,*}\theta^{*}\right)-\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}g(\theta_{\delta}^{r})\right)

\displaystyle=\theta^{0}-\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\delta\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}\left(\gamma\Gamma_{\delta}^{r}\theta_{\delta}^{r}+g(\theta_{\delta}^{r})+\gamma\sum_{q=0}^{r-1}R_{f,\delta}^{r,q}\theta_{\delta}^{q}\right)+\sqrt{\gamma}u_{\delta}^{t},

where
(*)
applies an identity analogous to (
117
). Similarly,
applying (
111
) and (
106
),

\displaystyle\xi_{\delta}^{t}=\sum_{q=0}^{t}W_{1}^{q}+\sum_{q=1}^{t}\sum_{r=0}^{q-1}(A^{q,r})_{11}Y_{1}^{r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=0}^{t}W_{1}^{q}+\sum_{q=1}^{t}\sum_{r=0}^{q-1}(\mathbb{E}\mathrm{D}_{r}V^{q})_{11}Y_{1}^{r}

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{=}\sum_{q=0}^{t}W_{1}^{q}+\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}Y_{1}^{r}={-}\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}f(\xi_{\delta}^{r},w^{*},\varepsilon)z_{\delta}^{r}+w_{\delta}^{t},

where
(*)
applies an identity analogous to (
116
). These
recursions are precisely (
88
–
89
).
Finally, by (
108
),
we note that
\{u_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
and
(\{w_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0},w_{\delta}^{*})
defined via (
111
) and (
118
) are
Gaussian with covariance

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[u_{\delta}^{t}\otimes u_{\delta}^{s}]

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{r=0}^{t}U_{1}^{r}\otimes\sum_{r=0}^{s}U_{1}^{r}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{r=0}^{t}Y_{1}^{t}\otimes\sum_{r=0}^{s}Y_{1}^{r}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{r=0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\xi_{\delta}^{r},w^{*},\varepsilon)z_{\delta}^{r}\otimes\sum_{r=0}^{s}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\xi_{\delta}^{r},w^{*},\varepsilon)z_{\delta}^{r}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[w_{\delta}^{t}\otimes w_{\delta}^{s}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{q=0}^{t}W_{1}^{q}\otimes\sum_{q=0}^{s}W_{1}^{q}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{q=0}^{t}V_{1}^{q}\otimes\sum_{q=0}^{s}V_{1}^{q}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\theta_{\delta}^{t}\otimes\theta_{\delta}^{s}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[w_{\delta}^{t}\otimes w_{\delta}^{*}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{q=0}^{t}W_{1}^{q}\otimes W_{2}^{0}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{q=0}^{t}V_{1}^{q}\otimes V_{2}^{0}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\theta^{t}\otimes\theta^{*}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[w_{\delta}^{*}\otimes w_{\delta}^{*}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2}^{0}\otimes W_{2}^{0}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[V_{2}^{0}\otimes V_{2}^{0}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\theta^{*}\otimes\theta^{*}],

which is precisely (
93
–
94
). This verifies
that the limits in (
112
) coincide with the definitions from
(
88
–
98
), showing the lemma.
∎

4.2.
Convergence to the continuous DMFT system

We now show that a continuous-time embedding of the preceding
discrete-time DMFT system converges to (the unique fixed
point of) the continuous-time DMFT system defined in Section

2.3
, as the discretization step size
\delta\to 0
.

To ease notation in this section, for all times
t\in[0,T]
,
we will use the time-discretization conventions

(119)

{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=\max\{i\delta:i\delta\leq t,i\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\}\in\delta\mathbb{Z}_{+},\quad{\lceil{t}\rceil}={\lfloor{t}\rfloor}+\delta\in\delta\mathbb{Z}_{+}

Let
\{\theta_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
,
\{\xi_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
,
and the correlation and response kernels

C_{\theta,\delta},R_{\theta,\delta},C_{f,\delta},R_{f,\delta},R_{f,\delta}^{*},\Gamma_{\delta}

be the components of the discrete-time DMFT system defined via the recurions
(
88
–
98
).
We define their continuous-time embeddings

\{\bar{\theta}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
,

\{\bar{\xi}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
,

\{\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}
,

\{\bar{C}_{f,\delta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}
,

\{\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t,*}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, and

\{\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
by

(120)

\bar{\theta}_{\delta}^{t}=\theta_{\delta}^{k},\;\bar{\xi}_{\delta}^{t}=\xi_{\delta}^{k},\;\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}=C_{\theta,\delta}^{k,j},\;\bar{C}_{f,\delta}^{t,s}=C_{f,\delta}^{k,j},\;\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t,*}=R_{f,\delta}^{k,*},\;\bar{\Gamma}_{f,\delta}^{t}=\Gamma_{f,\delta}^{k}\text{ if }{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=k\text{ and }{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}=j.

These embeddings are piecewise-constant and right-continuous with jumps at

\delta\mathbb{Z}_{+}
(henceforth abbreviated as
\delta
-p.c.r.c.)
We define also the
\delta
-p.c.r.c. embedding

\{\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}
by

\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }s>t\\
\mathrm{Id}&\text{ if }s\leq t\text{ and }{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}={\lfloor{t}\rfloor}\\
R_{\theta,\delta}^{k,j}&\text{ if }{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}=j<{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=k,\end{cases}

and a family of linear operators
\{\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

with
\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t}:L^{4}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{k})\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
by

(121)

\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t}(x)=\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t,s}x^{s}\mathrm{d}s,\quad\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t,s}=\delta^{-1}R_{f,\delta}^{k,j}\text{ if }{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}=j<{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=k.

We denote the tuples of these embedded correlation and response functions by

X^{\delta}=(\bar{C}_{f,\delta},\bar{R}_{f,\delta},\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{*},\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta}),\quad Y^{\delta}=(\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta},\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}).

The main result of this section is the following lemma, where we recall
the domain
\mathcal{S}(T,C_{0})
and metric
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\cdot,\cdot)
defined in Section

3
.

Lemma 4.3
.

(a)

For any sufficiently large constant
C_{0}\equiv C_{0}(T)>0
and any

\delta>0
,

(Y^{\delta},X^{\delta})\in\mathcal{S}(T,C_{0}).

(b)

For any sufficiently large constant
\lambda\equiv\lambda(T,C_{0})>0
,

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(X^{\delta},X)=0,\quad\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y^{\delta},Y)=0

where
(Y,X)\in\mathcal{S}^{\text{cont}}(T,C_{0})
is the fixed point given by Theorem

2.5
.

(c)

For any fixed
m\geq 0
and
0\leq t_{1}\leq\ldots\leq t_{m}\leq T
,
as
\delta\to 0
,

(122)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Law}(\bar{\theta}^{t_{1}}_{\delta},\ldots,\bar{\theta}^{t_{m}}_{\delta},\theta^{*})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\operatorname{Law}(\theta^{t_{1}},\ldots,\theta^{t_{m}},\theta^{*}),\,

(123)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Law}(\bar{\xi}_{\delta}^{t_{1}},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{\delta}^{t_{m}},w^{*},\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\operatorname{Law}(\xi^{t_{1}},\ldots,\xi^{t_{m}},w^{*},\varepsilon)

weakly and in Wasserstein-2.

Proof of Lemma
4.3
(a).

We define also the embeddings

(124)

\bar{u}_{\delta}^{t}=u_{\delta}^{k},\;\bar{w}_{\delta}^{t}=w_{\delta}^{k},\;\bar{r}_{f,\delta}^{t,*}=r_{f,\delta}^{k,*}\text{ if }{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=k,\quad\bar{r}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }s>t\\
\mathrm{Id}&\text{ if }s\leq t\text{ and }{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}={\lfloor{t}\rfloor}\\
r_{\theta,\delta}^{k,j}&\text{ if }{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}=k<{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=j.\end{cases}

Let
D([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
denote the space of
\delta
-p.c.r.c.
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-valued
processes
\{x^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, i.e. satisfying
x^{t}=x^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}

for all
t\in[0,T]
. We embed

r_{f,\delta}^{k,j}
as a family of linear operators

\bar{r}_{f,\delta}^{t}:D([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
given by

(125)

\bar{r}_{f,\delta}^{t}(x^{[t]})=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}r_{f,\delta}^{k,j}x^{j\delta}\text{ if }{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=k.

Finally, we identify the variables
\{z_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
of the
discrete-time DMFT system with the increments of the continuous-time Gaussian
or Poisson process
\{z^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
defined by
(
12
)/(
24
),

z_{\delta}^{t}=z^{(t+1)\delta}-z^{t\delta}.

Then the discrete-time DMFT recursions
(
88
–
98
)
imply the following equations for these embeddings:
For any
x\in D([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
,

(126)

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\delta}

\displaystyle=\bar{\theta}^{0}_{\delta}-\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}\Big({\gamma}\bar{\Gamma}^{r}_{\delta}\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta}+g(\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta})+{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r}(\bar{\theta}^{[r]}_{\delta})+{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r,*}\theta^{*}\Big)\mathrm{d}r+\sqrt{\gamma}\,\bar{u}^{t}_{\delta},

(127)

\displaystyle\bar{\xi}^{t}_{\delta}

\displaystyle={-}\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}}{\bar{\kappa}}\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}f(\bar{\xi}^{r-}_{\delta},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}+\bar{w}^{t}_{\delta},

\displaystyle\bar{r}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Id}-\mathbf{1}_{{\lceil{s}\rceil}\leq{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\int_{{\lceil{s}\rceil}}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}\bigg[\Big({\gamma}\bar{\Gamma}^{r}_{\delta}+\mathrm{D}g(\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta})\Big)\bar{r}_{\theta,\delta}^{r,s}+{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r}\Big(\bar{r}_{\theta,\delta}^{[r],s}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}r\text{ for }s\leq t,

(128)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999ptr_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}=0\text{ for }s>t,

(129)

\displaystyle\bar{r}_{f,\delta}^{t,*}

\displaystyle={-}\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\bar{\xi}^{t}_{\delta},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}}{\bar{\kappa}}\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}\bar{r}_{f,\delta}^{r-,*}\mathrm{d}z^{r}+\mathrm{D}_{w^{*}}f(\bar{\xi}^{t}_{\delta},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon),

(130)

\displaystyle\bar{r}_{f,\delta}^{t}(x^{[t]})

\displaystyle={-}\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\bar{\xi}^{t}_{\delta},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}}{\bar{\kappa}}\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}\bigg(\bar{r}_{f,\delta}^{r-}(x^{[r]})+\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\bar{\xi}^{r-}_{\delta},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)x^{r-}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z^{r},

and

(131)

\displaystyle\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\delta}\otimes\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\delta}]\text{ for }t,s\in[0,\infty)\cup\{*\}

(132)

\displaystyle\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\bar{r}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}]

(133)

\displaystyle\bar{C}_{f,\delta}^{t,s}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\bar{\xi}^{r-}_{\delta},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\otimes\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\bar{\xi}^{r-}_{\delta},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}}\right]

(134)

\displaystyle\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t}(x^{[t]})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\bar{r}_{f,\delta}^{t}(x^{[t]})\big]

(135)

\displaystyle\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t,*}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\bar{r}_{f,\delta}^{t,*}]

(136)

\displaystyle\bar{\Gamma}^{t}_{\delta}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{D}_{\xi}f(\bar{\xi}^{t}_{\delta},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)].

For example, since
\{\bar{\theta}_{\delta}^{t}\}
is
\delta
-p.c.r.c., we have
by (

) for
{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=k
that

\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t}(\bar{\theta}_{\delta}^{[t]})=\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t,s}\bar{\theta}_{\delta}^{s}\mathrm{d}s=\delta\sum_{q=0}^{k-1}(\delta^{-1}R_{f,\delta}^{k,q})\theta_{\delta}^{q}=\sum_{q=0}^{k-1}R_{f,\delta}^{k,q}\theta_{\delta}^{q}.

Then, since
\{\bar{\theta}_{\delta}^{t},\bar{u}_{\delta}^{t}\}
are both

\delta
-p.c.r.c. and defined by (

) and

), we have that (

) is equivalent to

),

\theta_{\delta}^{k}=\theta_{\delta}^{0}-\delta\sum_{r=0}^{k}\bar{\eta}^{r\delta}\Big(\gamma\Gamma_{\delta}^{r}\theta_{\delta}^{r}+g(\theta_{\delta}^{r})+\gamma\sum_{q=0}^{r-1}R_{f,\delta}^{r,q}\theta_{\delta}^{q}+\gamma R_{f,\delta}^{r,*}\theta^{*}\Big)+\sqrt{\gamma}u_{\delta}^{k}.

Similar arguments show that
(

–

) are equivalent to
(

–

), and multiplying
(

) on both sides by

). It is clear from the

–

–

), where (

)

) and (

).

Now let
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}
denote the space of all tuples

where

•

\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta}\equiv\{\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}
is a covariance kernel on
(\mathbb{R}^{k}\otimes[0,T])\times\mathbb{R}^{k^{*}}

that is
\delta
-p.c.r.c. in
(t,s)
.

\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}\equiv\{\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}
is a
\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
-valued process satisfying

,

\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}

\delta
-p.c.r.c. in
(t,s)
.

Likewise, let
\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}

-p.c.r.c. in

(t,s)
,

•

\mathbb{R}^{k\times k^{*}}

\delta
-p.c.r.c. in

,

•

\bar{R}_{f,\delta}\equiv\{\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
is a
family of linear operators
\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t}:D([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{k})\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
having
the forms, for some matrix-valued coefficients

,

(137)

\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t}(x^{[t]})=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}R_{f,\delta}^{k,j}x^{j\delta}\text{ if }{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=k.

Given any
X^{\delta}=(\bar{C}_{f,\delta},\bar{R}_{f,\delta},\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{*},\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta})\in\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}
, note that

\{\bar{u}_{\delta}^{t}\}\sim\operatorname{GP}(0,\bar{C}_{f,\delta})
is
\delta
-p.c.r.c. with
probability 1. Then it may be checked inductively in
k=0,1,2,\ldots
that
(
126
) and (
128
) have
unique solutions over
t,s\in[0,k\delta)
, which are
\delta
-p.c.r.c. Then

Y^{\delta}=(\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta},\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta})
defined by
(
131
–

) belongs to
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}
,
i.e. (

), (

), and

–

) define a mapping

\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{D}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}}:\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}.

Similarly, given any

, it may be checked inductively that
(

) and (

) have unique

\delta
-p.c.r.c. in

, and that there exist (random)

, the solution to (

) takes a form

\bar{r}_{f,\delta}^{t}(x^{[t]})=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}r_{f,\delta}^{k,j}x^{j\delta}\text{ if }{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=k.

Then
X^{\delta}=(\bar{C}_{f,\delta},\bar{R}_{f,\delta},\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{*},\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta})
defined by
(

–
136
) belongs to
\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}
,
i.e. (

),
(

–

), and
(

–

\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\xi}}:\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}.

These mappings may be understood as
discretized versions of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}
and
\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}
from Section

3
. Now denoting by

X^{\delta}=(\bar{C}_{f,\delta},\bar{R}_{f,\delta},\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{*},\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta}),\quad Y^{\delta}=(\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta},\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta})

the specific elements of
\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}
that
correspond to the previously defined embeddings of the discrete-time DMFT
recursions,
since these satisfy (

–
