), they
are a fixed point of these mappings, i.e.

\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{D}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}}(X^{\delta})=Y^{\delta},\quad\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\xi}}(Y^{\delta})=X^{\delta}.

Lemma
4.3
(a) then holds by the following
reasoning: On one hand,
(X^{\delta},Y^{\delta})\in\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}\times\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}
is the unique such
fixed point of these mappings, because the equations
(
126
–
136
) uniquely determine,
recursively in time,

\begin{gathered}\{\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s},\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s:{\lfloor{t}\rfloor},{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}=0}\Rightarrow\{\bar{C}_{f,\delta}^{t,s},\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t},\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t,*},\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t,s:{\lfloor{t}\rfloor},{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}=0}\Rightarrow\\
\{\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s},\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s:{\lfloor{t}\rfloor},{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}\in\{0,\delta\}}\Rightarrow\{\bar{C}_{f,\delta}^{t,s},\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t},\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t,*},\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t,s:{\lfloor{t}\rfloor},{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}\in\{0,\delta\}}\Rightarrow\\
\{\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s},\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,s}\}_{t,s:{\lfloor{t}\rfloor},{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}\in\{0,\delta,2\delta\}}\Rightarrow\{\bar{C}_{f,\delta}^{t,s},\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t},\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t,*},\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t,s:{\lfloor{t}\rfloor},{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}\in\{0,\delta,2\delta\}}\Rightarrow\ldots\end{gathered}

On the other hand, let us extend each operator

\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t}:D([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{k})\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
having the form
(
137
) to an operator

\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t}:L^{4}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{k})\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
given by

\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t}(x^{[t]})=\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t,s}x^{s}\mathrm{d}s,\quad\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t,s}=\delta^{-1}R_{f,\delta}^{k,j}\text{ if }{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}=j<{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=k.

In particular, this extension of
\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{t}:D([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{k})\to\mathbb{R}^{k}

defined via (
134
) and
(
125
) is precisely the embedded operator we have defined in
(
121
). In this way,
we may identify
\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
as a subset of the
space
\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
of Section
3
, where the
discontinuity set
D\subset[0,T]
of Definition
3.1

is
\delta\mathbb{Z}_{+}\cap[0,T]
.

Then for any sufficiently large constants
C_{0}\equiv C_{0}(T)>0

and
\lambda\equiv\lambda(T,C_{0})>0
,
the same argument as in
3.3
shows that
if
X^{\delta}\in\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
, then

Y^{\delta}=\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{D}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}}(X^{\delta})
defined via
(
126
), (
128
), and
(
131
–
132
) satisfies

Y^{\delta}\in\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
;
the same argument as in
3.4
shows that if

Y^{\delta}\in\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
, then

X^{\delta}=\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\xi}}(Y^{\delta})
defined via
(
127
),
(
129
–
130
), and
(
133
–
136
) satisfy

X^{\delta}\in\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
;
and the same arguments as in Lemmas
3.6
and

3.8
show that the combined map

\mathcal{T}^{\delta}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{D}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}}\circ\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\xi}}

is contractive in the metric
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\cdot,\cdot)
on

\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
. The conditions that

(C_{\theta},R_{\theta})\in\mathcal{S}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
are
\delta
-p.c.r.c. and
hence belong to
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}
are closed under this metric, so

\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
is also complete
in
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\cdot,\cdot)
. Then by the Banach fixed-point theorem,
there exists a fixed point of
\mathcal{T}^{\delta}:\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})\to\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
,
which must coincide with
Y^{\delta}

by the above claim of uniqueness of this fixed point in
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}
.
This shows that
Y^{\delta}\in\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
, and hence also

X^{\delta}\in\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
,
i.e.
(Y^{\delta},X^{\delta})\in\mathcal{S}(T,C_{0})
as claimed.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.3
(b).

We compare the outputs of the continuous map
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}

and the discretized map
\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{D}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}}
when both are
given the same discrete input
X^{\delta}\in\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
. Given any such input

X^{\delta}=(\bar{C}_{f,\delta},\bar{R}_{f,\delta},\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{*},\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta})
, consider the continuous
process
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
and the embedded discrete process

\{\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\delta}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
coupled using the same underlying
realization of
\{\bar{u}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}\sim\operatorname{GP}(0,\bar{C}_{f,\delta})
:

\displaystyle\theta^{t}

\displaystyle=\theta^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}\Big({\gamma}\bar{\Gamma}^{r}_{\delta}\theta^{r}+g(\theta^{r})+{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r}(\theta^{[r]})+{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r,*}\theta^{*}\Big)\mathrm{d}r+\sqrt{\gamma}\,\bar{u}^{t}_{\delta}\,,

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\delta}

\displaystyle=\theta^{0}-\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}\Big({\gamma}\bar{\Gamma}^{r}_{\delta}\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta}+g(\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta})+{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r}(\bar{\theta}^{[r]}_{\delta})+{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r,*}\theta^{*}\Big)\mathrm{d}r+\sqrt{\gamma}\,\bar{u}^{t}_{\delta}\,.

Then

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\delta}-\theta^{t}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{\eta}^{r}\left({{\gamma}\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta}^{r}(\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta}-\theta^{r})+(g(\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta})-g(\theta^{r}))+{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r}\left({\left({\bar{\theta}_{\delta}-\theta}\right)^{[r]}}\right)}\right)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\left({\bar{\eta}^{r}-\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}}\right)\left({g(\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta})+{\gamma}\left({\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta}^{r}\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta}+\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r}\left({{\bar{\theta}_{\delta}}^{[r]}}\right)+\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r,*}\theta^{*}}\right)}\right)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}\Big({\gamma}\bar{\Gamma}^{r}_{\delta}\theta^{r}+g(\theta^{r})+{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r}(\theta^{[r]})+{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r,*}\theta^{*}\Big)\mathrm{d}r.

As a consequence,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\|\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\delta}-\theta^{t}\|^{2}}\right]\leq 4((\mathrm{I})+(\mathrm{II})+(\mathrm{III})+(\mathrm{IV}))
where

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{\eta}^{r}\left({{\gamma}\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta}^{r}(\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta}-\theta^{r})+(g(\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta})-g(\theta^{r}))}\right)\mathrm{d}r\right\|^{2},

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{\eta}^{r}{{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r}\left({\left({\bar{\theta}_{\delta}-\theta}\right)^{[r]}}\right)}\mathrm{d}r\right\|^{2},

\displaystyle(\mathrm{III})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\left({\bar{\eta}^{r}-\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}}\right)\left({g(\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta})+{\gamma}\left({\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta}^{r}\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\delta}+\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r}\left({{\bar{\theta}_{\delta}}^{[r]}}\right)+\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r,*}\theta^{*}}\right)}\right)\mathrm{d}r\right\|^{2},

\displaystyle(\mathrm{IV})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{r}\Big({\gamma}\bar{\Gamma}^{r}_{\delta}\theta^{r}+g(\theta^{r})+{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r}(\theta^{[r]})+{\gamma}\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{r,*}\theta^{*}\Big)\mathrm{d}r\right\|^{2}.

By the Lipschitz continuity of
g(\cdot)
and bound (
61
)
for
\bar{R}_{f,\delta}
, the first two terms can be bounded by

(\mathrm{I})+(\mathrm{II})\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda}e^{2\lambda t}\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda s}\mathbb{E}\left[{\|\theta^{s}-\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\delta}\|^{2}}\right]

where
\lesssim
denotes inequality up to a constant not depending on
\delta
.
For the third term, by the Lipschitz continuity of

\{\bar{\eta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

in Assumption
2.1
, we have
\int_{0}^{T}(\bar{\eta}^{r}-\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}})^{2}\mathrm{d}r\lesssim\delta^{2}
for a constant
C>0
, and hence also

(\mathrm{III})\lesssim\delta^{2}
by Cauchy-Schwarz. Finally
(\mathrm{IV})\lesssim\delta^{2}

by the conditions for
X^{\delta}\in\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0})
. Then
choosing large enough
\lambda
yields
\sup_{s\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda s}\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{s}-\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\delta}\|^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}
, which implies

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{\theta},\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta})\lesssim\delta
.
A similar argument shows
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(R_{\theta},\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta})\lesssim\delta
, establishing

(138)

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}(X^{\delta}),\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{D}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}}(X^{\delta}))\lesssim\delta\text{ for all }X^{\delta}\in\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\xi}(T,C_{0}).

Analogously, we may compare
\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}

and
\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\xi}}
given the same discrete input
Y^{\delta}=(\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta},\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta})\in\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
. Coupling by the same realizations of
(\{\bar{w}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]},w^{*}_{\delta})\sim\operatorname{GP}(0,\bar{C}_{\theta,\delta})
an
\{z^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, we have

\displaystyle\xi^{t}

\displaystyle={-}\int_{0}^{{t}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{r}}}{\bar{\kappa}}\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}f(\xi^{r-},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}+\bar{w}^{t}_{\delta}\,,

\displaystyle\bar{\xi}^{t}_{\delta}

\displaystyle={-}\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}}{\bar{\kappa}}\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}f(\bar{\xi}^{r-}_{\delta},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}+\bar{w}^{t}_{\delta}\,.

Then

\displaystyle\xi^{t}-\bar{\xi}^{t}_{\delta}

\displaystyle=-\underbrace{\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{r}}}{\bar{\kappa}}\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}\left({f(\xi^{r-},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)-f(\bar{\xi}^{r-}_{\delta},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)}\right)\mathrm{d}z^{r}}_{(\mathrm{I})}

\displaystyle\qquad-\underbrace{\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}-\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}}{\bar{\kappa}}R_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}{f(\bar{\xi}^{r-}_{\delta},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}z^{r}}_{(\mathrm{II})}-\underbrace{\int_{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{r}}}{\bar{\kappa}}\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}^{t,r}f(\xi^{r-},w_{\delta}^{*},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}}_{(\mathrm{III})}.

We can bound
\mathbb{E}[\|\xi^{t}-\bar{\xi}^{t}_{\delta}\|^{2}]\leq 3(\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{I})^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{II})^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{III})^{2}])
.
For
\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{I})^{2}]
, by using Lemma
A.1
, boundedness of
\bar{R}_{\theta,\delta}
, and Lipschitz continuity of
f
, as in the above argument, we have

\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{I})^{2}]\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda}e^{2\lambda t}\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda s}\mathbb{E}\left[{\|\xi^{s}-\bar{\xi}^{s}_{\delta}\|^{2}}\right].

Using Lemma
A.1
, we have similarly to the proof of (
138
) that
\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{II})^{2}]\lesssim\delta^{2}
and
\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{III})^{2}]\lesssim\delta
up to some constants not depending on
\delta
. Then choosing
\lambda
large enough and rearranging shows
\sup_{s\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda s}\mathbb{E}\|\xi^{s}-\bar{\xi}^{s}_{\delta}\|^{2}\lesssim\delta
. Then by an analogous argument,

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f},\bar{C}_{f,\delta})^{2}\lesssim\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{r}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\xi^{r-},w^{*}_{\delta},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}-\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{r}\rfloor}}}{\bar{\kappa}}f(\bar{\xi}^{r-}_{\delta},w^{*}_{\delta},\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|^{2}\lesssim\delta\,,

that is,

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f},\bar{C}_{f,\delta})\lesssim\delta^{1/2}
.
Similarly, one may analyze
R_{f}
and
R_{f}^{*}
to show that

(139)

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}(Y^{\delta}),\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\xi}}(Y^{\delta}))\lesssim\delta^{1/2}\text{ for all }Y^{\delta}\in\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}(T,C_{0}),

and we omit this verification for brevity.

Now let
Y^{\delta}\in\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\delta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}(T,C_{0})
be the fixed
point of
\mathcal{T}^{\delta}
, and let
Y\in\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}(T,C_{0})
be the fixed
point of
\mathcal{T}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y,Y^{\delta})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\mathcal{T}(Y),\mathcal{T}^{\delta}(Y^{\delta}))\leq\underbrace{\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\mathcal{T}(Y),\mathcal{T}(Y^{\delta}))}_{\mathrm{(I)}}+\underbrace{\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\mathcal{T}(Y^{\delta}),\mathcal{T}^{\delta}(Y^{\delta}))}_{\mathrm{(II)}}.

By the contractivity (
86
) of
\mathcal{T}
, we can choose

\lambda
large enough such that

(140)

\displaystyle\mathrm{(I)}\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y,Y^{\delta})\,.

To control
\mathrm{(II)}
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}\circ\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}(Y^{\delta}),\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{D}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}}\circ\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\xi}}(Y^{\delta}))

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}\circ\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}(Y^{\delta}),\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}\circ\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\xi}}(Y^{\delta}))

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}}\circ\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\xi}}(Y^{\delta}),\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{D}_{\xi}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}}\circ\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\xi}}(Y^{\delta}))\lesssim\delta^{1/2}

where the last inequality holds
by (
138
), (
139
), and Lemma

3.6
. Thus
\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y,Y^{\delta})\lesssim\delta^{1/2}
.
Setting
X=\mathcal{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{S}_{\xi}}(Y)
and
X^{\delta}=\mathcal{T}^{\delta}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\theta}\to\mathcal{D}_{\xi}}(Y^{\delta})
, this
implies by Lemma
3.8
that also

\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(X,X^{\delta})\lesssim\delta^{1/2}
, so

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(X,X^{\delta})=0,\qquad\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(Y,Y^{\delta})=0.

∎

Proof of Lemma
4.3
(c).

The convergence
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathsf{dist}_{\lambda,T}(C_{f},\bar{C}_{f,\delta})=0

implies that there exists a coupling of
\{u^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}\sim\operatorname{GP}(0,C_{f})
and
\{\bar{u}^{t}_{\delta}\}_{t\in[0,T]}\sim\operatorname{GP}(0,\bar{C}_{f,\delta})
for which

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\|u^{t}-\bar{u}^{t}_{\delta}\|^{4}=0.

Then defining
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
via
(C_{f},R_{f},R_{f}^{*},\Gamma)

and
\{\bar{\theta}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
via

(\bar{C}_{f,\delta},\bar{R}_{f,\delta},\bar{R}_{f,\delta}^{*},\bar{\Gamma}_{\delta})
using this coupling of
\{u^{t}\}
and
\{\bar{u}_{\delta}^{t}\}
, the same arguments as leading to (
81
) show

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-4\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{t}-\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\delta}\|^{4}=0.

In particular,

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{E}[\|\theta^{t_{1}}-\bar{\theta}^{t_{1}}_{\delta}\|^{2}+\ldots+\|\theta^{t_{m}}-\bar{\theta}^{t_{m}}_{\delta}\|^{2}]=0
, which implies the Wasserstein-2 convergence
(
122
). A similar argument shows
(
123
).
∎

5.
Discretization Error of SGD and SME

In this section, we conclude the proof of Theorem
2.6
by
analyzing the original SGD/SME dynamics (
7
) and
(
8
), and
showing that they are also well-approximated by the discrete-time dynamics
(
87
) as
\delta\to 0
.

5.1.
Discretization of SGD

Recall the SGD update

\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{k+1}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{k}=-\eta^{k}{\left(\frac{1}{\kappa}\sum_{i\in S^{k}}\mathbf{x}_{i}\otimes f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{k},\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*},\varepsilon_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\,g(\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{k})\right)},\qquad|S^{k}|=\kappa,

where
g(\cdot)
is applied row-wise. We will analyze a Poissonized
version of this process: Recall
\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}=\{S\subset[n]:|S|=\kappa\}
, and let

\mathbf{N}^{t}=\{N_{S}^{t}\}_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}

be a
\mathbb{R}^{\binom{n}{\kappa}}
-valued process indexed by
\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}
, whose
coordinates are independent homogeneous Poisson jump processes with rate

n^{1-\alpha}/{n\choose\kappa}
. Set

N^{t}=\mathbf{1}^{\top}\mathbf{N}^{t}=\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}N_{S}^{t}.

Marginally,
N^{t}
is a Poisson jump process with rate
n^{1-\alpha}
. We define
a Poissonized version of the SGD dynamics
\{\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{k}\}_{k\geq 0}
of
(
6
) (without time rescaling) by

(141)

\bm{\nu}^{t}=\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{N^{t}},\qquad\bm{\nu}^{0}=\bm{\theta}^{0}.

Then
\bm{\nu}^{t}
is a continuous-time jump process given by

\displaystyle\bm{\nu}^{t}

\displaystyle=\bm{\nu}^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\eta^{N^{s-}}\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\left({\frac{1}{\kappa}\sum_{i\in S}\mathbf{x}_{i}\otimes f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*},\varepsilon_{i})+\frac{1}{n}g(\bm{\nu}^{s-})}\right)\mathrm{d}N^{s}_{S}

\displaystyle=\bm{\nu}^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\eta^{N^{s-}}\left({\frac{1}{\kappa}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{i}\otimes f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*},\varepsilon_{i})\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\mathbf{1}_{i\in S}\mathrm{d}N^{s}_{S}+\frac{1}{n}g(\bm{\nu}^{s-})\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\mathrm{d}N^{s}_{S}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bm{\nu}^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\eta^{N^{s-}}\left({\frac{1}{\kappa}\,\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}))+\frac{1}{n}g(\bm{\nu}^{s-})\mathrm{d}N^{s}}\right).

Here
f(\cdot)
is also applied row-wise, and
\bm{\Pi}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times{n\choose\kappa}}
is the incidence matrix with entries
\Pi_{i,S}=\mathbf{1}_{i\in S}
.

Fixing a discretization step size
\delta>0
, in this section, we continue
to write
{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}
and
{\lceil{t}\rceil}
for the time discretization notation
previously defined in (
119
). We then consider an approximation

\{\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
to the above Poissonized process, defined by

(142)

\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}=\bm{\nu}^{0}-\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}g(\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s.

It may be checked that
\{\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is a
piecewise-constant embedding of the discrete-time dynamics
(
87
) in the SGD setting of

\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{k}=\bm{\Pi}\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{k}
,

\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}=\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{k}\text{ if }{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=k,

upon identifying
\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{k}=\int_{k\delta}^{(k+1)\delta}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s}=\mathbf{N}^{(k+1)\delta}-\mathbf{N}^{k\delta}
.

The main result of this section is the following comparison lemma.

Lemma 5.1
.

For any fixed
T>0
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
and some constant
C\equiv C(T,\alpha)>0
not depending on
\delta
,
almost surely as
n,d\to\infty
,

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{\|\bm{\nu}^{t}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\|^{2}}{d}\leq C\delta.

We proceed to prove Lemma
5.1
.
The following first establishes a high-probability bound on
the norm of
\{\bm{\nu}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, which
will be important for controlling error terms in the subsequent analysis.

Lemma 5.2
.

For any fixed
T>0
and some constant
C\equiv C(T,\alpha)>0
,
almost surely for all large
n,d
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bm{\nu}^{t}\|\leq C\sqrt{n}.

Proof.

We continue to write
\|\cdot\|
for the vector
\ell_{2}
-norm and matrix
Frobenius norm, denote
\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\infty}=\max_{i,j}|M_{ij}|
, and write
\lesssim

for inequality up to a constant possibly depending on
T,\alpha
.

Using the boundedness of
f(\cdot)
and Lipschitz continuity of
g(\cdot)
in
Assumption
2.3
and the scalings
\eta^{t}\lesssim n^{\alpha}

and
\kappa\asymp n^{\alpha}
in Assumption
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{\nu}^{t}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bm{\nu}^{0}\|+\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\right\|+n^{\alpha-1}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}g(\bm{\nu}^{s-})\mathbf{1}^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bm{\nu}^{0}\|+\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s}\right\|\|f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\|_{\infty}+n^{\alpha-1}\int_{0}^{t}\|g(\bm{\nu}^{s-})\|\mathbf{1}^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bm{\nu}^{0}\|+\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\bm{\Pi}\,\mathbf{N}^{T}\|+n^{\alpha-1}\int_{0}^{t}(\|\bm{\nu}^{s-}\|+\sqrt{n})\mathrm{d}N^{s}.

Here,
\mathbf{N}^{T}
has i.i.d. coordinates with law

\operatorname{Poisson}(Tn^{1-\alpha}/\binom{n}{\kappa})
. Then
applying the concentration inequality (
99
) to

Q=\|\bm{\Pi}\,\mathbf{N}^{T}\|^{2}
, where

\mathbb{E}Q=\left(\frac{Tn^{1-\alpha}}{\binom{n}{\kappa}}\right)^{2}\|\bm{\Pi}\mathbf{1}\|^{2}+\frac{Tn^{1-\alpha}}{\binom{n}{\kappa}}\operatorname{Tr}\bm{\Pi}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}=\left(\frac{Tn^{1-\alpha}}{\binom{n}{\kappa}}\right)^{2}\cdot n\binom{n-1}{\kappa-1}^{2}+\frac{Tn^{1-\alpha}}{\binom{n}{\kappa}}\binom{n}{\kappa}\kappa\lesssim n,

\mu_{0}\lesssim\mathbb{E}Q\lesssim n
, and
\mu_{2}\lesssim\|\bm{\Pi}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}\|_{\infty}\leq\kappa\lesssim n^{\alpha}
, we have
Q\lesssim n
a.s. for all large

n,d
. Since
N^{T}=\mathbf{1}^{\top}\mathbf{N}^{T}\sim\operatorname{Poisson}(n^{1-\alpha})
where

\alpha<1
, by a standard Poisson tail bound, also

N^{T}\lesssim n^{1-\alpha}
a.s. for all large
n,d
.
Under Assumption
2.2
,

\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim 1
a.s. for all large
n,d
, and

\|\bm{\nu}^{0}\|=\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|\lesssim\sqrt{n}
. Then on the
intersection
\mathcal{E}
of these events, which holds a.s. for all large
n,d
,

\|\bm{\nu}^{t}\|\lesssim\sqrt{n}+n^{\alpha-1}\int_{0}^{t}\|\bm{\nu}^{s-}\|\mathrm{d}N^{s}.

Letting
t_{1},t_{2},\ldots
be the jumps of
\{N^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, this means
that for a constant
C\equiv C(T,\alpha)>0
,

\|\bm{\nu}^{t_{k+1}}\|\leq C\sqrt{n}+Cn^{\alpha-1}(\|\bm{\nu}^{t_{1}}\|+\ldots+\|\bm{\nu}^{t_{k}}\|).

A discrete Grönwall inequality then shows that

\|\bm{\nu}^{t_{k}}\|\leq C\sqrt{n}e^{Ckn^{\alpha-1}}
for all
k=1,\ldots,N^{T}
.
Since the number of jumps
N^{T}

satisfies
N^{T}\lesssim n^{1-\alpha}
on
\mathcal{E}
, this shows the lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

Let

\mathbf{Z}^{t}=\mathbf{N}^{t}-t\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{{n\choose\kappa}}\mathbf{1}_{n\choose\kappa},\qquad Z^{t}=\mathbf{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}^{t}

be the centered versions of
\mathbf{N}^{t}
and
N^{t}=\mathbf{1}^{\top}\mathbf{N}^{t}
, where
\mathbf{1}_{n\choose\kappa}
denotes the
all-1’s vector in
\mathbb{R}^{n\choose\kappa}
. Then

(143)

\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s}=\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s}+n^{1-\alpha}\frac{\binom{n-1}{\kappa-1}}{\binom{n}{\kappa}}\mathbf{1}_{n\choose\kappa}\mathrm{d}s=\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s}+\kappa n^{-\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{n\choose\kappa}\mathrm{d}s

where we have used

\bm{\Pi}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n\choose\kappa}={n-1\choose\kappa-1}\mathbf{1}_{n\choose\kappa}
. We introduce an intermediary process

(144)

\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}=\bm{\nu}^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-\int_{0}^{t}{\bar{\eta}^{s}}g(\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s})\mathrm{d}s

and proceed to first compare
\{\bm{\nu}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
to
\{\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, and then compare
\{\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
to the final
discretized process
\{\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
.

Step 1: Bounding
\|\bm{\nu}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}\|

We decompose the difference into four terms

\displaystyle\bm{\nu}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\eta^{N^{s-}}}{\kappa}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})}_{\mathrm{(I)}+\mathrm{(II)}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{s}g(\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s})\mathrm{d}s-\int_{0}^{t}\eta^{N^{s-}}\frac{1}{n}g(\bm{\nu}^{s-})\mathrm{d}N^{s}}_{\mathrm{(III)}+\mathrm{(IV)}}\,,

applying (
143
) and
\mathrm{d}N^{s}=\mathrm{d}Z^{s}+n^{1-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}s

to separate
(\mathrm{I})+(\mathrm{II})
and
(\mathrm{III})+(\mathrm{IV})
into their drift and martingale
components:

\displaystyle\mathrm{(I)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}(f(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}))\kappa n^{-\alpha}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt-\int_{0}^{t}\left({\frac{\eta^{N^{s}}}{\kappa}-\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}}\right)\mathbf{X}^{\top}f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\kappa n^{-\alpha}\mathrm{d}s\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{(II)}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s})(f(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}))}_{\mathrm{(II.a)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt-\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\left({\frac{\eta^{N^{s-}}}{\kappa}-\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}}\right)\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})}_{\mathrm{(II.b)}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{(III)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{s}g(\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s})\mathrm{d}s-\int_{0}^{t}{\frac{\eta^{N^{s-}}}{n^{\alpha}}g(\bm{\nu}^{s-})}\mathrm{d}s\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{(IV)}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\eta^{N^{s-}}\frac{1}{n}g(\bm{\nu}^{s-})\mathrm{d}Z^{s}.

By Doob’s
L^{p}
-maximal inequality and a standard bound for the central moments
of a Poisson random variable, for any fixed
p>1
,

\mathbb{E}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\frac{N^{t}}{n^{1-\alpha}}-t|^{p}\lesssim\mathbb{E}|\frac{N^{T}}{n^{1-\alpha}}-T|^{p}\lesssim n^{-(1-\alpha)p/2}
. Thus,
fixing any sufficiently small constant
\iota>0
,
Markov’s inequality and the Borel-Cantelli lemma imply
that a.s. for all large
n,d
,

(145)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\frac{Z^{t}}{n^{1-\alpha}}\right|=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\frac{N^{t}}{n^{1-\alpha}}-t\right|\leq n^{-\frac{1-\alpha}{2}+\iota}<n^{-\iota}.

By the scaling and continuity assumptions for

\kappa,\eta
in Assumption
2.1
, we then
have for any fixed
p\geq 1
that

(146)

\int_{0}^{T}\left|{\frac{\eta^{N^{s}}}{\kappa}-\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}}\right|^{p}\mathrm{d}s=\int_{0}^{T}\left|{\frac{\bar{\eta}^{N^{s}/n^{1-\alpha}}}{\bar{\kappa}}-\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}}\right|^{p}\mathrm{d}s+o(1)=o(1).

Thus a.s. for all large
n,d
, by the boundedness and Lipschitz continuity of
f
,

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{(I)}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}\|\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s}-\bm{\nu}^{s}\|\mathrm{d}s+\|\mathbf{X}\|\sqrt{n}\int_{0}^{t}\left|{\frac{\eta^{N^{s}}}{\kappa}-\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}}\right|\mathrm{d}s

(147)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s}-\bm{\nu}^{s}\|\mathrm{d}s+o(\sqrt{n}).

Since
g
is Lipschitz and
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bm{\nu}^{t}\|\lesssim\sqrt{n}

a.s. for all large
n,d
from
5.2
,
we can similarly bound

(148)

\|(\mathrm{III})\|\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s}-\bm{\nu}^{s}\|\mathrm{d}s+o(\sqrt{n}).

Also
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\eta^{t}\lesssim n^{\alpha}
by Assumption
2.1
,
and
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|g(\bm{\nu}^{t})\|\lesssim\sqrt{n}
a.s. for all
large
n,d
by
5.2
and the Lipschitz continuity of
g
, so

(149)

\|(\mathrm{IV})\|\lesssim n^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}Z^{s}\lesssim n^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{t}|\lesssim n^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot n^{\iota+\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}\lesssim n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}+\iota}=o(\sqrt{n}),

the last bound holding for
\iota>0
small enough.

For the term
(\mathrm{II})
, let
\{\mathbf{E}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be any
\mathbb{R}^{m\times\binom{n}{\kappa}}
-valued predictable process, and define

\mathbf{M}^{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s}.

By Itô’s formula, for any continuously-differentiable function
F:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}
,

(150)

F(\mathbf{M}^{t})-F(\mathbf{0})=\int_{0}^{t}\nabla F(\mathbf{M}^{s-})^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{M}^{s}+\sum_{0<s\leq t}(F(\mathbf{M}^{s})-F(\mathbf{M}^{s-})-\nabla F(\mathbf{M}^{s-})^{\top}\Delta\mathbf{M}^{s})

where
\Delta\mathbf{M}^{s}=\mathbf{M}^{s}-\mathbf{M}^{s-}
. In particular,

\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2}=2\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbf{M}^{s-}}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s}+\sum_{0<s\leq t}\|\Delta\mathbf{M}^{s}\|^{2}\,.

Here, each jump of
\mathbf{M}^{s}
occurs at a jump of
N_{S}
for some
S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}
, in
which case
\Delta\mathbf{M}^{s}=\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathbf{e}_{S}
where
\mathbf{e}_{S}
is the standard basis
vector corresponding to the coordinate
S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}
. Hence

\displaystyle\sum_{0<s\leq t}\|\Delta\mathbf{M}^{s}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathbf{e}_{S}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{N}^{s}_{S}=\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathbf{e}_{S}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}Z^{s}_{S}+\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{{n\choose\kappa}}\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathbf{e}_{S}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{Tr}\mathbf{E}^{s}\operatorname{diag}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s}){\mathbf{E}^{s}}^{\top}+\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{{n\choose\kappa}}\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{Tr}\mathbf{E}^{s}{\mathbf{E}^{s}}^{\top}\mathrm{d}s\,.

Thus

\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2}=\underbrace{\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{{n\choose\kappa}}\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{Tr}\mathbf{E}^{s}{\mathbf{E}^{s}}^{\top}\mathrm{d}s}_{:=P^{t}}+\underbrace{2\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbf{M}^{s-}}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{Tr}\mathbf{E}^{s}\operatorname{diag}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s}){\mathbf{E}^{s}}^{\top}}_{:=Q^{t}}.

We apply this to the first component
\mathrm{(II.a)}
of
\mathrm{(II)}
,
identifying
\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}\equiv\mathbb{R}^{m}
with
m=dk
and noting that

\mathrm{(II.a)}=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s})(f(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}))=\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s}

where

\mathbf{E}^{s}=\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{A}^{s}\bm{\Pi},\qquad\mathbf{A}^{s}=[\mathbf{a}_{1}^{s},\ldots,\mathbf{a}_{n}^{s}]\in\mathbb{R}^{dk\times n},

\mathbf{a}_{i}^{s}=\operatorname{vec}(\mathbf{x}_{i}\otimes[f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})]).

This gives the decomposition

(151)

\|\mathrm{(II.a)}\|^{2}=P^{t}+Q^{t}.

For this choice of
\mathbf{E}^{s}
, we may bound

P^{t}
using
\bm{\Pi}\bm{\Pi}^{\top}=({n-1\choose\kappa-1}-{n-2\choose\kappa-2})\mathrm{Id}_{n}+{n-2\choose\kappa-2}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}
,
where
{n-1\choose\kappa-1}-{n-2\choose\kappa-2}=(1-\frac{\kappa-1}{n-1})\binom{n-1}{\kappa-1}\lesssim\frac{\kappa}{n}\binom{n}{\kappa}\lesssim n^{\alpha-1}\binom{n}{\kappa}
and

\binom{n-2}{\kappa-2}\lesssim\frac{\kappa^{2}}{n^{2}}\binom{n}{\kappa}\lesssim n^{2\alpha-2}\binom{n}{\kappa}
. Then

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\mathbf{E}^{s}{\mathbf{E}^{s}}^{\top}

\displaystyle\leq\left({\frac{\bar{\eta}^{t}}{\bar{\kappa}}}\right)^{2}\left(\binom{n-1}{\kappa-1}-\binom{n-2}{\kappa-2}\right)\operatorname{Tr}\mathbf{A}^{s}{\mathbf{A}^{s}}^{\top}+\left({\frac{\bar{\eta}^{t}}{\bar{\kappa}}}\right)^{2}\binom{n-2}{\kappa-2}\|\mathbf{A}^{s}\mathbf{1}_{n}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{\alpha-1}\binom{n}{\kappa}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}\|f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+n^{2\alpha-2}\binom{n}{\kappa}\|\mathbf{X}^{\top}(f(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{\alpha-1}\binom{n}{\kappa}\|f(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\|^{2}\lesssim n^{\alpha-1}\binom{n}{\kappa}\|\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s-}-\bm{\nu}^{s-}\|^{2}

where the last two inequalities hold on a
\mathbf{X}
-dependent event a.s. for all
large
n
. So

(152)

P_{t}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s}-\bm{\nu}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s.

For the martingale part
Q^{t}
, we may write

Q^{t}=\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbf{H}^{s}}^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s},\quad\mathbf{H}^{s}=(H_{S}^{s})_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}},\quad H_{S}^{s}=2{\mathbf{M}^{s-}}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathbf{e}_{S}+\|\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathbf{e}_{S}\|^{2}.

Then by the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy type inequality of

[
48
, Theorem 2.11]
,
identifying
\mathbf{Z}^{s}
as a compensated Poisson random measure on
(0,T]\times\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}
, for any
p\geq 1
and some constant
C_{p}>0
,

(153)

\mathbb{E}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(Q^{t})^{2p}\leq C_{p}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathbf{H}^{s}\|^{2}\,\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{\binom{n}{\kappa}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{p}+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}|H_{S}^{s}|^{2p}\,\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{\binom{n}{\kappa}}\mathrm{d}s\right).

To bound
\mathbf{H}^{s}
, note that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathbf{e}_{S}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbf{A}^{s}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\|\bm{\Pi}\mathbf{e}_{S}\|\lesssim\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|f\|_{\infty}\cdot\sqrt{\kappa}\lesssim n^{\alpha/2},

and the operator norm of this matrix can be bounded similarly by

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{E}^{s}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|f\|_{\infty}\cdot\|\bm{\Pi}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\sqrt{\|\bm{\Pi}\|_{\ell_{1}\to\ell_{1}}\|\bm{\Pi}\|_{\ell_{\infty}\to\ell_{\infty}}}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa\binom{n-1}{\kappa-1}}\lesssim{\kappa}\sqrt{\frac{{n\choose\kappa}}{n}}\lesssim n^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{{n\choose\kappa}}{n}}.

Thus
|H_{S}^{s}|\lesssim\|\mathbf{M}^{s-}\|\,n^{\alpha/2}+n^{\alpha}
, and

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}^{s}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\left[{\left({{\mathbf{M}^{s-}}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathbf{e}_{S}}\right)^{2}+n^{2\alpha}}\right]\lesssim\left({{\mathbf{M}^{s-}}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{s}{\mathbf{E}^{s}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}^{s-}+n^{2\alpha}{n\choose\kappa}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left({\|\mathbf{M}^{s-}\|^{2}n^{2\alpha-1}{n\choose\kappa}+n^{2\alpha}{n\choose\kappa}}\right)\,.

As a consequence, applying these bounds and Hölder’s inequality to
(
153
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(Q^{t})^{2p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left({n^{\alpha}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathbf{M}^{s-}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+n^{\alpha+1}}\right)^{p}+\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{{n\choose\kappa}}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\left({\|\mathbf{M}^{s-}\|n^{\alpha/2}+n^{\alpha}}\right)^{2p}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{\alpha p}\,\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathbf{M}^{s}\|^{2p}\mathrm{d}s+n^{p(\alpha+1)}+n^{1-\alpha}\left(n^{\alpha p}\,\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathbf{M}^{s}\|^{2p}\mathrm{d}s+n^{2\alpha p}\right)

(154)

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{\alpha p+1-\alpha}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{s}\|^{2p}\mathrm{d}s+n^{\alpha p+p}

for
p\geq 1
. To bound
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{s}\|^{2p}
, observe that taking expectations
in Itô’s formula (
150
) and recalling that each jump of

\mathbf{M}^{s}
is given by
\nabla\mathbf{M}^{s}=\mathbf{E}^{s}\mathbf{e}_{S}
, corresponding to the jump of
some coordinate
N_{S}
which has rate
\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{\binom{n}{\kappa}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}F(\mathbf{M}^{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{{n\choose\kappa}}\mathbb{E}\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\left({F(\mathbf{M}^{t-}+\mathbf{E}^{t}\mathbf{e}_{S})-F(\mathbf{M}^{t-})-\nabla F(\mathbf{M}^{t-})^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{t}\mathbf{e}_{S}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{{n\choose\kappa}}\mathbb{E}\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-h)\,\mathbf{e}_{S}^{\top}{\mathbf{E}^{t}}^{\top}\cdot\nabla^{2}F(\mathbf{M}^{t-}+h\mathbf{E}^{t}\mathbf{e}_{S})\cdot\mathbf{E}^{t}\mathbf{e}_{S}\mathrm{d}h

(155)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{n}{{n\choose\kappa}}\mathbb{E}\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-h)\|\nabla^{2}F(\mathbf{M}^{t-}+h\mathbf{E}^{t}\mathbf{e}_{S})\|\mathrm{d}h\,.

Applying this with
F(\mathbf{x})=\|\mathbf{x}\|^{2p}
, we know
\|\nabla^{2}F(\mathbf{x})\|\lesssim\|\mathbf{x}\|^{2p-2}
. Thus

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{n}{{n\choose\kappa}}\mathbb{E}\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-h)\|\mathbf{M}^{t-}+h\mathbf{E}^{t}\mathbf{e}_{S}\|^{2p-2}\mathrm{d}h\lesssim\frac{n}{{n\choose\kappa}}\mathbb{E}\sum_{S\in\mathscr{S}_{\kappa}}\left({\|\mathbf{M}^{t-}\|^{2p-2}+\|\mathbf{E}^{t}\mathbf{e}_{S}\|^{2p-2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim n\left({\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{t-}\|^{2p-2}+n^{\alpha(p-1)}}\right)\lesssim n(\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{t-}\|^{2p})^{\frac{p-1}{p}}+n^{\alpha(p-1)+1}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{t-}\|^{2p}+n^{p}.

Equivalently, in integral form,

\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2p}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{s}\|^{2p}+n^{p}}\right)\mathrm{d}s
.
Then by Grönwall’s inequality,
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2p}\lesssim n^{p}
.
Applying this to (
154
) shows
\mathbb{E}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(Q^{t})^{2p}\lesssim n^{(\alpha+1)p+(1-\alpha)}
. For any fixed and sufficiently small
\iota>0
, choosing
p\geq 1

large enough and applying Markov’s inequality and the Borel-Cantelli lemma, this
implies that a.s. for all large
n,d
,

(156)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Q^{t}|<n^{\frac{\alpha+1}{2}+\iota}=o(n).

Applying (
152
) and (
156
) to (
151
),
this shows

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{(II.a)}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\bm{\nu}^{s}-\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+o(n).

For the second term
\mathrm{(II.b)}
, we have analogously

\mathrm{(II.b)}=\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}^{s}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}^{s}

where now

\mathbf{E}^{s}=\left(\frac{\eta^{N^{s-}}}{\kappa}-\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}\right)\mathbf{A}^{s}\bm{\Pi},\qquad\mathbf{A}^{s}=[\mathbf{a}_{1}^{s},\ldots,\mathbf{a}_{n}^{s}]\in\mathbb{R}^{dk\times n},\qquad\mathbf{a}_{i}^{s}=\operatorname{vec}(\mathbf{x}_{i}\otimes f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})).

Then we have an analogous decomposition
\|\mathrm{(II.b)}\|^{2}=P^{t}+Q^{t}
. For

P^{t}
, similar arguments as above show

P^{t}\lesssim\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{\binom{n}{\kappa}}\cdot n^{\alpha-1}\binom{n}{\kappa}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{\eta^{N^{s-}}}{\kappa}-\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}\right)^{2}\|f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s=o(n),

the last statement applying boundedness of
f
and
(
146
). For
Q^{t}
, applying

|\frac{\eta^{N^{s-}}}{\kappa}-\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}|\lesssim 1

in the bounds for
\mathbf{E}^{s}
, arguments identical to the above show

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Q^{t}|<n^{\frac{\alpha+1}{2}+\iota}=o(n).

Combining these bounds for
\mathrm{(II.a)}
and
\mathrm{(II.b)}
shows, on an
event holding a.s. for all large
n,d
,

(157)

\|\mathrm{(II)}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\bm{\nu}^{s}-\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+o(n).

Then combining (
147
), (
157
), (
148
),
and (
149
), we have
\|\bm{\nu}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\bm{\nu}^{s}-\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+o(n)
, and hence
by Grönwall’s inequality,

(158)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bm{\nu}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}\|^{2}\lesssim o(n).

Step 2: Bounding
\|\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\|

Next, we compare
\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}
to the discretized process
\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}

\displaystyle=\bm{\nu}^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-\int_{0}^{t}{\bar{\eta}^{s}}g(\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s})\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}

\displaystyle=\bm{\nu}^{0}-\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}g(\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s.

We may decompose

\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}=\mathrm{(I)}+\mathrm{(II)}+\mathrm{(III)}+\mathrm{(IV)}+\mathrm{(V)}+\mathrm{(VI)}

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{(I)}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\lfloor t\rfloor}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s})\big(f(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\big),

\displaystyle\mathrm{(II)}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\lfloor t\rfloor}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}-\bar{\eta}^{\lfloor s\rfloor}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{(III)}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\lfloor t\rfloor}\bar{\eta}^{s}\big(g(\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s})-g(\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s})\big)\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathrm{(IV)}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\lfloor t\rfloor}\big(\bar{\eta}^{s}-\bar{\eta}^{\lfloor s\rfloor}\big)g(\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathrm{(V)}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\bar{\kappa}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\bm{\Pi}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s-},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{(VI)}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{s}g(\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s})\mathrm{d}s.

Note that
t-{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}\lesssim\delta
, and
by the Lipschitz continuity of
\bar{\eta}
in Assumption
2.1
,
for any
p\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
,

(159)

\int_{0}^{t}|\bar{\eta}^{s}-\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}|^{p}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\delta.

Then on the event
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|g(\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t})\|\leq C\sqrt{n}
which holds a.s. for all large
n,d
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{(III)}\|\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s}\|\mathrm{d}s,\qquad\|\mathrm{(IV)}\|^{2}\lesssim\delta\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s}-\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+n\right),\qquad\|\mathrm{(VI)}\|\lesssim\delta\sqrt{n}.

For the remaining stochastic integration terms, applying
(
159
) and similar arguments as in Step 1 (omitted here for
brevity), on an event holding a.s. for all large
n,d
,

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{(I)}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+o(n),\qquad\|\mathrm{(II)}\|^{2}\lesssim\delta n+o(n),\qquad\|\mathrm{(V)}\|^{2}\lesssim\delta n+o(n).

Combining this gives

\|\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s}-\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\delta n+o(n)
,
so Grönwall’s inequality implies

(160)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\nu}}^{t}\|^{2}\lesssim\delta n+o(n).

The lemma follows from combining these bounds (
158
) and
(
160
).
∎

5.2.
Discretization of SME

We now apply a similar analysis to the Stochastic Modified Equation. The
SME process (
8
) may be written concisely as

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{t}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{s}\left({\mathbf{X}^{\top}f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})+g(\bm{\theta}^{s})}\right)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})

where
\mathbf{B}^{s}
is a standard
n
-dimensional Brownian motion.
We compare this to the discretized process
\{\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

defined by

(161)

\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}=\bm{\theta}^{0}-\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}\left({\mathbf{X}^{\top}f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})+g(\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s})}\right)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}}{\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}).

One may again check that
\{\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is precisely a
piecewise-constant embedding of the discrete-time dynamics
(
87
) in the SME setting of
\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{k}\sim\mathcal{N}(\delta\bar{\kappa}\,\mathbf{1}_{n},\delta\bar{\kappa}\,\mathrm{Id}_{n})
,

\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}=\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{k}\text{ if }{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}=k,

upon identifying
\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{k}=\int_{k\delta}^{(k+1)\delta}(\bar{\kappa}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}^{t})
.

The following lemma is analogous to Lemma
5.1
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For any fixed
T>0
,
\delta\in(0,1)
, and some constant
C\equiv C(T)>0
not depending on
\delta
, almost surely as
n,d\to\infty
,

(162)

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\|^{2}}{d}\leq C\delta.

Proof.

We decompose the difference
\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}
into six terms:

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}

\displaystyle=-\underbrace{\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}\left({\mathbf{X}^{\top}(f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}))+g(\bm{\theta}^{s})-g(\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s})}\right)\mathrm{d}s}_{(\mathrm{I})}

\displaystyle\qquad-\underbrace{\int_{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}\left({\mathbf{X}^{\top}f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})+g(\bm{\theta}^{s})}\right)\mathrm{d}s}_{(\mathrm{II})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}}{\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}^{s})\big(f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\big)}_{(\mathrm{III})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\int_{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}}{\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})}_{(\mathrm{IV})}

\displaystyle\qquad-\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\left({\bar{\eta}^{{s}}-\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}}\right)\left({\mathbf{X}^{\top}f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})+g(\bm{\theta}^{s})}\right)\mathrm{d}s}_{\mathrm{(V)}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{s}}-\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}}{\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})}_{\mathrm{(VI)}}\,,

Bounding the martingale terms (III) and (IV)

Identify
\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}\equiv\mathbb{R}^{m}
for
m=dk
, and denote

\mathbf{M}^{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}^{s})\mathbf{D}^{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{dk},\qquad\mathbf{D}^{s}=\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}}}{\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}}}\big(f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\big).

Note that this may be written as

\mathrm{d}\mathbf{M}^{t}=\mathbf{A}^{t}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}^{t},\qquad\mathbf{A}^{t}=[\mathbf{a}_{1}^{t},\ldots,\mathbf{a}_{n}^{t}]\in\mathbb{R}^{dk\times n},

\mathbf{a}_{i}^{t}=\frac{\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}}{\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}}}\operatorname{vec}(\mathbf{x}_{i}\otimes[f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s},\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})]).

By Itô’s formula,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2}=\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{A}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}2{\mathbf{M}^{s}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{s}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}^{s}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(\bar{\eta}^{{\lfloor{s}\rfloor}})^{2}}{\bar{\kappa}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}\cdot\|f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s},\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}s}_{:=P^{t}}+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{s}2{\mathbf{M}^{s}}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}^{s})\mathbf{D}_{s}}_{:=Q^{t}}.

By the Lipschitz continuity of
f
, on an event holding a.s. for all large

n,d
, we have

P^{t}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s.

By the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality

[
48
, Theorem 2.11]
, for any
p\geq 1
,

(163)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(Q^{t})^{2p}\leq C_{p}\,\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|{\mathbf{M}^{s}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{p}.

Applying
\|\mathbf{A}^{s}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|f\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
, this implies

\mathbb{E}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(Q^{t})^{2p}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{s}\|^{2p}\mathrm{d}s.

Then applying Itô’s formula to
F(\mathbf{x})=\|\mathbf{x}\|^{2p}
, since
\mathbf{M}^{t}
has no drift
coefficient and

\nabla^{2}F(\mathbf{x})=2p\|\mathbf{x}\|^{2p-2}\mathrm{Id}+4p(p-1)\|\mathbf{x}\|^{2p-4}\mathbf{x}\mathbf{x}^{\top}
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[{\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2p-2}\|\mathbf{A}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2p-4}\|{\mathbf{A}^{t}}\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2}}\right]

(164)

\displaystyle\lesssim n\,\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2p-2}\lesssim n(\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2p})^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2p}+n^{p}.

Thus by Grönwall’s inequality,

\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2p}\lesssim n^{p}
, implying that

\mathbb{E}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(Q^{t})^{2p}\lesssim n^{p}
.
Then Markov’s inequality shows that
for any fixed and sufficiently small
\iota>0
, a.s. for all large
n,d
,

(165)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Q^{t}|<n^{\frac{1}{2}+\iota}=o(n).

Applying these bounds gives

\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\bm{\theta}_{s}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+o(n).

Then

(166)

\|(\mathrm{III})\|^{2}=\|\mathbf{M}^{{\lfloor{t}\rfloor}}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+o(n).

Similarly, a.s. for all large
n,d
,

(167)

\|(\mathrm{IV})\|^{2}\lesssim\delta n+o(n).

Bounding the drift terms (I) and (II)

Write

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{t}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\eta}^{s}\left({\mathbf{X}^{\top}f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})+g(\bm{\theta}^{s})}\right)\mathrm{d}s+\mathbf{M}^{t},

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}^{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\bar{\eta}^{s}}{\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\operatorname{diag}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}^{s})f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}).

The same argument as above shows, on an event holding a.s. for all large
n,d
,
for every
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{M}^{t}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|f(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+o(n)\lesssim n.

Then by the boundedness of
f(\cdot)
and Lipschitz continuity of
g(\cdot)
, also

\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\bm{\theta}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+n
, so Grönwall’s
inequality gives

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|^{2}\lesssim n.

This implies, by the Lipschitz continuity of
f(\cdot)
and
g(\cdot)
, on an event
holding a.s. for all large
n,d
,

(168)

\|(\mathrm{I})\|\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s}\|\mathrm{d}s,\qquad\|(\mathrm{II})\|\lesssim\delta\sqrt{n}.

Bounding the discretization terms (V) and (VI)

Applying (
159
) and similar arguments as for
the preceding bounds for
\mathrm{(II)}
and
\mathrm{(IV)}
, on an event
holding a.s. for all large
n,d
, we have

(169)

\|\mathrm{(V)}\|^{2}\lesssim\delta n,\qquad\|\mathrm{(VI)}\|^{2}\lesssim\delta n+o(n).

Combining (
166
), (
167
),
(
168
), and (
169
),

\displaystyle\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{s}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\delta n+o(n),

and thus Grönwall’s inequality yields
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\|^{2}\lesssim\delta n+o(n)
, implying the lemma.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.6
(a).

Let
\bm{\theta}^{t}=\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{\lfloor tn^{1-\alpha}\rfloor}
be the time-rescaled
SGD process in (
7
) (where here
{\lfloor{\cdot}\rfloor}
denotes the
usual integer part), and let
\bm{\nu}^{t}=\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{N^{t}}
be
the Poissonized SGD process in (
141
). For any
\delta>0
,
let
\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}
be the discretized approximation in (
142
).
Applying an induction over discrete time increments of
\delta
to
(
142
), we see that
\{\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

is piecewise-constant with jumps at
\delta\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, taking values

(170)

\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}=\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{k}\text{ for all }t\in[k\delta,(k+1)\delta)

where
\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{k}
are the iterations of the discrete-time dynamics
(
87
) defined using the Poisson variables

\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{k}=\bm{\Pi}\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{k}

and
\mathbf{P}_{\delta}^{k}=\int_{k\delta}^{(k+1)\delta}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{N}^{s}
.

Fix any
T>0
. For each
t\in[0,T]
, set

\tau(t)=\inf\{\tau\geq 0:N^{\tau}\geq\lfloor tn^{1-\alpha}\rfloor\}
,
so that

\bm{\theta}^{t}=\bm{\nu}^{\tau(t)}.

Note that the bound (
145
) implies that for some

\iota>0
, on an event holding a.s. for all large
n,d
,

(171)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\tau(t)-t|\lesssim n^{-\iota}.

Fix any
0\leq t_{1}\leq\ldots\leq t_{m}\leq T
, and fix any

\delta\in(0,1)
such that none of
t_{1},\ldots,t_{m}
is an integer multiple of

\delta
. Then a.s. for all large
n,d
, the piecewise-constant nature of

\{\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
and (
171
) imply that

\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{\tau(t_{i})}=\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t_{i}}
for each
i=1,\ldots,m
. Then
by Lemma
5.1
, a.s. for all large
n,d
,

\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\|\bm{\theta}^{t_{i}}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t_{i}}\|^{2}}{d}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau(t_{i})}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{\tau(t_{i})}\|^{2}}{d}\lesssim\delta.

This implies that

(172)

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}W_{2}\left(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{t_{1}},\ldots,\theta_{j}^{t_{m}},\theta_{j}^{*})},\,\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\nu_{\delta,j}^{t_{1}},\ldots,\nu_{\delta,j}^{t_{m}},\theta_{j}^{*})}\right)^{2}\lesssim\delta.

Let
k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}
be the integers for which
t_{i}\in[k_{i}\delta,(k_{i}+1)\delta)
for each
i=1,\ldots,m
.
Then by
(
170
) and Lemma
4.1
,

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}W_{2}\left(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\nu_{\delta,j}^{t_{1}},\ldots,\nu_{\delta,j}^{t_{m}},\theta_{j}^{*})},\,\operatorname{Law}(\theta_{\delta}^{k_{1}},\ldots,\theta_{\delta}^{k_{m}},\theta^{*})\right)=0

where the right side denotes the joint law under the discrete DMFT system of
Section
4.1
. Recalling the embedding
(
120
) of this discrete system, where

\bar{\theta}_{\delta}^{t_{i}}=\theta_{\delta}^{k_{i}}
for each
i=1,\ldots,m
, this is
equivalently written as

(173)

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}W_{2}\left(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\nu_{\delta,j}^{t_{1}},\ldots,\nu_{\delta,j}^{t_{m}},\theta_{j}^{*})},\,\operatorname{Law}(\bar{\theta}_{\delta}^{t_{1}},\ldots,\bar{\theta}_{\delta}^{t_{m}},\theta^{*})\right)=0.

Finally, by Lemma
4.3
(c),

(174)

\lim_{\delta\to 0}W_{2}\Big(\operatorname{Law}(\bar{\theta}_{\delta}^{t_{1}},\ldots,\bar{\theta}_{\delta}^{t_{m}},\theta^{*}),\,\operatorname{Law}(\theta^{t_{1}},\ldots,\theta^{t_{m}},\theta^{*})\Big)=0

where the right side denotes the joint law under the continuous DMFT system
defined via the fixed point of
Theorem
2.6
. Combining (
172
),
(
173
), and (
174
) and taking the limit
n,d\to\infty
followed by
\delta\to 0
, we obtain (
29
).
The argument for (
30
) is the same.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.6
(b).

Let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the SME process (
8
), and let

\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}
be its discretized approximation (
161
).
Again by induction over discrete time increments of
\delta
, we see that

\{\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

is piecewise-constant with jumps at
\delta\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, taking values

(175)

\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t}=\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{k}\text{ for all }t\in[k\delta,(k+1)\delta)

where
\bm{\theta}_{\delta}^{k}
are the iterations of the discrete-time SME dynamics
(
87
) defined using the Gaussian variables

\mathbf{z}_{\delta}^{k}=\int_{k\delta}^{(k+1)\delta}(\bar{\kappa}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}^{t})
.
By Lemma
5.3
, a.s. for all large
n,d
,

\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\|\bm{\theta}^{t_{i}}-\bm{\nu}_{\delta}^{t_{i}}\|^{2}}{d}\lesssim\delta.

The proof is then completed as above, using
Lemmas
4.1
and

4.3
(c) for the SME.
∎

Appendix A
Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a complete right-continuous filtration on
the probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
. Let

\{X^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be a
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-predictable process taking
values in any Euclidean space
\mathbb{R}^{m}
. If
\{z^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

is the
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-adapted diffusion process (
24
),
then for any
p\geq 1
,
there exists a constant
C_{p}>0
such that for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left\|\int_{0}^{s}X^{r}\,\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|_{2}^{p}\leq C_{p}\,\mathbb{E}\left(\bar{\kappa}\int_{0}^{t}\|X^{r}\|_{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\right)^{p}+C_{p}\,\mathbb{E}\left(\bar{\kappa}\int_{0}^{t}\|X^{r}\|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\right)^{p/2}.

If
\{z^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
is the
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-adapted Poisson process
(
12
), then for any
p\geq 1
,
there exists a constant
C_{p}>0
such that for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left\|\int_{0}^{s}X^{r}\,\mathrm{d}z^{r}\right\|_{2}^{p}\leq C_{p}\,\mathbb{E}\left(\bar{\kappa}\int_{0}^{t}\|X^{r}\|_{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\right)^{p}+C_{p}\,\mathbb{E}\left(\bar{\kappa}\int_{0}^{t}\|X^{r}\|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\right)^{p/2}+C_{p}\bar{\kappa}\,\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\|X^{r}\|_{2}^{p}\mathrm{d}r.

Proof.

This follows from
[
48
, Thm 2.11]
applied to

\int_{0}^{s}X^{r}\mathrm{d}z^{r}=\int_{0}^{s}X^{r}(\mathrm{d}z^{r}-\bar{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}r)+\int_{0}^{s}X^{r}\bar{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}r
,
where
\mathrm{d}z^{r}-\bar{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}r
is either
\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}}\,\mathrm{d}b^{r}
for a
standard Brownian motion
\{b^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
, or a compensated Poisson
process with rate
\bar{\kappa}
.
∎
